 ## Security rules (public repository)
 
 GitHub's own guidance is that self-hosted runners should not be used with public repositories,
 because a pull request can run arbitrary code on them. We use them anyway, under these rules:
 
+**Who can reach the runners at all** (owner, 2026-11-06, "A";
+[decisions](decisions.md)). On lambdaclass/concrete every org member
+has push: 44 accounts. A push of any branch, or a `workflow_dispatch`, runs the workflow file of that
+branch, so any of them can put a job on these runners with no approval; the rules below do not
+change that. The owner accepted this exposure, as for stabileo's runners. On concrete2 one account
+had push.
+
 1. **No `pull_request` event runs on a self-hosted runner.** The `runs-on` expression enforces this
    for the workflow as written. But a pull request runs the workflow file **from the PR branch**, so
    a fork PR can rewrite `runs-on` to `[self-hosted]` and the guard is gone. The guard prevents
@@ -114,7 +120,7 @@ because a pull request can run arbitrary code on them. We use them anyway, under
    stay on the machine. Isolation comes from destroying the VM or container after each job and
    starting a fresh one from a clean image, which registers a new ephemeral runner. Automate that
    with actions-runner-controller, or with a supervisor **outside** the job's VM or container that
-   calls `gh api -X POST repos/unbalancedparentheses/concrete2/actions/runners/registration-token`.
+   calls `gh api -X POST repos/lambdaclass/concrete/actions/runners/registration-token`.
    The supervisor's admin token must never be readable from inside a job. Persistent runners (or
    `--ephemeral` re-registered on the same machine, which is the same thing) are acceptable only
    with rule 2, the cleanup hook below and a dedicated low-privilege user, and with the

 succeeded in 0ms:
---
description: Hand a validated branch to the landing train (scripts/land/train.sh); never push main by hand
argument-hint: "<branch or ref> [train name]"
---

Land: $ARGUMENTS

Rules: [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md). The manual is
[docs/LANDING.md](../../docs/LANDING.md); `scripts/land/train.sh` (header) is the
contract. Landings reach `origin` (lambdaclass/concrete, the one repository) only through it.

**Where.** Run `train.sh` from a full clone with the repository hook enabled (`git clone`, then
`git config core.hooksPath .githooks`), never from a linked worktree: it refuses both a worktree and
a clone with the hook off (ISS-0015, LANDING.md "Where a train runs"). Use one clone per train, from
`build` through `land` (the train's state lives in that clone's git directory), and no other push
from it while the train runs. If you cannot, do not land: report the evidence below to the orchestrator. Never fall back to
pushing `main` by hand.

**Preconditions** (stop and report the missing one):
- the branch is pushed as `wip/<branch>` and that SHA is the one you validated;
- `/creview` finished (clean, or 5 rounds with follow-ups recorded);
- the fast and area gates passed at that SHA; a full set ran on the server or in CI if the change
  is broad;
- bug records, `changelog.d/` fragment and regenerated views are in the branch;
- a branch that changes a language rule goes in its own train, ahead of work that depends on it.

**If you are the landing orchestrator** (the user or the orchestrator asked you to land):

```sh
scripts/land/train.sh build <name> origin/wip/<branch> [more refs]   # train/<name> = origin/main + refs
scripts/land/train.sh check <name>     # hook selection; full set on the server via concrete-remote
scripts/land/train.sh ci    <name>     # push ci/<name>, dispatch CI
scripts/land/train.sh watch <name>     # verdict, failing job log tails
scripts/land/train.sh land --dry-run <name> && scripts/land/train.sh land <name>
```

Each step stops on any failure; a conflict outside generated views exits 3 and names the files,
which you resolve on the member branch (`/rebase`), not on the train. A moved `origin/main`
means `build --force` and a new run: a result belongs to the exact SHA that ran. A red CI run: read
the logs, fix on the member branch, rebuild. Never land on a timeout or a skipped job.

**Otherwise** do not land: report the branch, head SHA and the evidence above, so the orchestrator
puts it on a train.

After a landing: `gh run list --workflow CI --branch main` and confirm the run for the landed SHA
is green; a red main stops the line.
name: CI

# The root's CI after the legacy/ move. Eighteen jobs, all required (required-checks.txt):
#   Landing record  main's push run names the run that qualified its exact SHA (R-0570), kept from
#                the base unchanged; when it finds one, the jobs below are skipped on main.
#   Root checks  every check of scripts/checks/run.sh: legacy/ frozen, the day-one root inventory,
#                workflow YAML, Markdown links, the landing script and readiness, the review tool.
#   Spec build   lake build of the specification, warnings refused.
#   Proof axioms, Proof trust ban (sorry, native_decide), Kernel replay (leanchecker), External
#                re-check (con-leche, nanoda)  the proof-integrity checks of scripts/proofs.
#   Slice 0 backends, artifacts, trusted base, reproducibility (x86-64 Linux and AArch64 macOS)
#                the compiler's gates (scripts/checks/compiler), each in the compiler's devshell.
#   Oracle (GitHub-hosted fetch + smoke)  the published oracle fetched, verified and run on a
#                GitHub-hosted runner (ISS-0005, P13; R-0564), kept from the base unchanged.
# legacy/ is never built or run here: its gates were qualified at tag legacy-compiler-final. The old
# workflows are archived, unrun, under legacy/.github/workflows.
# A train's dispatch names its base: the run's title then says which base it was built on, and
# main's Landing record accepts a run only for the base main had before the push (R-0570).
run-name: ${{ github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch' && inputs.base != '' && format('{0} at base {1}', github.ref_name, inputs.base) || '' }}

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
  pull_request:
  workflow_dispatch:
    inputs:
      base:
        # THE BASE OF A TRAIN'S CANDIDATE, passed by train.sh ci (-f base=<sha>). The root has no
        # base-sensitive check yet; the title records the base so main's Landing record can require
        # that the run it accepts was built on the base main had before the landing.
        description: "Base SHA of a train's candidate (recorded in the run title)"
        type: string
        required: false
        default: ""

concurrency:
  group: ci-${{ github.ref }}-${{ github.event_name }}
  cancel-in-progress: ${{ github.event_name == 'push' || github.event_name == 'pull_request' }}

# Every run step executes in the gate userland (flake.nix, `gates` shell), which marks itself with
# CONCRETE_GATES_SHELL=1; scripts/checks/lib/gate_env.sh refuses anywhere else. It contains no Lean:
# lean-action's elan toolchain is the one on PATH in the spec job.
defaults:
  run:
    shell: "nix develop .#gates --command bash --noprofile --norc -eo pipefail {0}"

jobs:
  # MAIN'S RUN NAMES THE RUN THAT QUALIFIED ITS EXACT SHA (R-0570), as in the base's workflow. On a
  # push to main this job looks up, with train.sh record, a completed dispatch of this workflow at
  # exactly this SHA and at the base main had before the push, with every required job succeeded.
  # Found: every other job is skipped and the run links it. Not found, or any other event: every job
  # runs. Each job below needs it and carries the gate; check_landing_script.sh holds both.
  landing-record:
    name: Landing record
    runs-on: ${{ (github.event_name != 'pull_request' && vars.CONCRETE_SELF_HOSTED == 'true') && fromJSON('["self-hosted","linux","x64","concrete"]') || 'ubuntu-latest' }}
    timeout-minutes: 15
    permissions:
      actions: read
      contents: read
    outputs:
      recorded: ${{ steps.record.outputs.recorded }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
        if: github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'

      - name: Install Nix (GitHub-hosted only)
        if: github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' && runner.environment != 'self-hosted'
        uses: cachix/install-nix-action@v31
        with:
          extra_nix_config: |
            experimental-features = nix-command flakes

      - name: "Look up the run that qualified this exact SHA (none found: every job runs here)"
        id: record
        if: github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'
        env:
          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
          BEFORE: ${{ github.event.before }}
          HEAD_SHA: ${{ github.sha }}
          LAND_REPO: ${{ github.repository }}
          LAND_NO_NIX: "1"
        run: |
          recorded=false run=""
          if out="$(git fetch -q --depth=1 origin "$BEFORE" 2>&1 \
                    && bash scripts/land/train.sh record "$HEAD_SHA" --base "$BEFORE" --exclude-run "$GITHUB_RUN_ID" 2>&1)"; then
            run="$(printf '%s\n' "$out" | sed -n 's/^qualifying_run=\([0-9][0-9]*\)$/\1/p' | tail -1)"
          fi
          printf '%s\n' "$out"
          if [ -n "$run" ]; then
            recorded=true
            { echo "### Landing record"
              echo "Run [$run](https://github.com/$LAND_REPO/actions/runs/$run) passed every landing-tier and required job at exactly \`$HEAD_SHA\`."
              echo "This run skips those jobs: that run is the result for this SHA."; } >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
          else
            { echo "### No landing record"
              echo "No run qualifies \`$HEAD_SHA\`, so every job runs here:"
              printf '\n```\n%s\n```\n' "$out"; } >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
          fi
          echo "recorded=$recorded" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"

  root-checks:
    name: Root checks
    needs: landing-record
    if: ${{ !cancelled() && needs.landing-record.outputs.recorded != 'true' }}
    # Self-hosted runners never take pull_request events (forks could run code on them).
    runs-on: ${{ (github.event_name != 'pull_request' && vars.CONCRETE_SELF_HOSTED == 'true') && fromJSON('["self-hosted","linux","x64","concrete"]') || 'ubuntu-latest' }}
    steps:
      # Full history: legacy_frozen.sh reads the move commit named in scripts/checks/legacy.lock.
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0

      - name: Install Nix (GitHub-hosted only)
        if: runner.environment != 'self-hosted'
        uses: cachix/install-nix-action@v31
        with:
          extra_nix_config: |
            experimental-features = nix-command flakes

      - name: Root checks (scripts/checks/run.sh)
        run: bash scripts/checks/run.sh

  spec:
    name: Spec build
    needs: landing-record
    if: ${{ !cancelled() && needs.landing-record.outputs.recorded != 'true' }}
    runs-on: ${{ (github.event_name != 'pull_request' && vars.CONCRETE_SELF_HOSTED == 'true') && fromJSON('["self-hosted","linux","x64","concrete"]') || 'ubuntu-latest' }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Install Nix (GitHub-hosted only)
        if: runner.environment != 'self-hosted'
        uses: cachix/install-nix-action@v31
        with:
          extra_nix_config: |
            experimental-features = nix-command flakes

      - name: Install Lean (via lean-action)
        id: lean_install
        # A toolchain download can fail on a dropped connection; one paused retry keeps a network
        # blip from failing (and hiding) the whole run.
        continue-on-error: true
        uses: leanprover/lean-action@v1
        with:
          build-args: "--log-level=warning"

      - name: Wait before retrying the Lean install
        if: steps.lean_install.outcome == 'failure'
        run: sleep 60

      - name: Install Lean (via lean-action) (retry)
        if: steps.lean_install.outcome == 'failure'
        uses: leanprover/lean-action@v1
        with:
          build-args: "--log-level=warning"

      - name: Build the specification, warnings refused
        run: |
          OUTPUT=$(lake build 2>&1) || { echo "$OUTPUT"; exit 1; }
          if printf '%s\n' "$OUTPUT" | grep -i "warning"; then
            echo "::error::Build produced warnings"
            printf '%s\n' "$OUTPUT"
            exit 1
          fi

    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: cachix/install-nix-action@v31
      - name: Compare every stage in distinct build directories
        run: nix develop --command timeout 3600 bash scripts/checks/compiler/slice0_repro.sh manifest.txt
      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: reproducibility-${{ matrix.id }}
          path: manifest.txt
          if-no-files-found: error

  compare-linux:
    name: Cross-machine reproducibility (x86-64 Linux)
    needs: reproducibility
    # Runs only when every reproducibility job ran (landing-record gate inherited); no Nix here.
    defaults:
      run:
        shell: bash
    runs-on: ubuntu-24.04
    permissions:
      contents: read
    steps:
      - uses: actions/download-artifact@v4
        with:
          pattern: reproducibility-linux-*
          path: manifests
      - name: Compare like targets, including compiler and toolchain identity
        run: |
          set -eu
          m1=manifests/reproducibility-linux-first/manifest.txt
          m2=manifests/reproducibility-linux-independent/manifest.txt
          # Both manifests carry the identity and at least one artifact hash.
          for m in "$m1" "$m2"; do grep -q '^compiler_commit=' "$m"; grep -q '/lcb.exe$' "$m"; done
          diff -u "$m1" "$m2"
# Agent and contributor workflow

Status: operational procedure. How work is done in this repository: the agent playbooks, the
rules they share, and [concurrent work](#concurrent-work-one-worktree-per-writer) (one worktree per
writer, the server gate runs, publishing). It absorbed `CONCURRENT_WORK.md` on 2026-10-29. The
tool manuals it points to stay separate: [LANDING.md](docs/LANDING.md) (landing trains),
[REVIEW.md](docs/REVIEW.md) (`scripts/review/review.sh`), [WORK_QUEUE.md](legacy/docs/archive/roadmap-2026-11/WORK_QUEUE.md) (the agent work
queue), [STATUS_PAGE.md](legacy/docs/project/STATUS_PAGE.md) and [PROMOTION_PIPELINE.md](legacy/docs/project/PROMOTION_PIPELINE.md) (the
validation stages, CI tiers and result vocabulary).

**Since the legacy/ move (R-0558).** The old compiler and its gates are frozen in `legacy/` and never
run. Where this document names a path that is not at the root (`scripts/tests/`, `scripts/gen/`,
`docs/bugs/`, `Concrete/` and the like), it now lives under `legacy/` and describes the old
compiler. The checks a push, CI and the train run are `scripts/checks/run.sh`. A defect in the
spec, the proofs, the tooling or CI is a row in [ISSUES.tsv](ISSUES.tsv); `/file-bug` and `/fix-bug`
are archived with the old bug ledger (`legacy/.claude/commands/`).

## Playbooks

Project playbooks live in `.claude/commands/`. Every agent session, on the laptop or the server,
runs the same procedure by name instead of receiving a long prompt:

| command | what it does |
|---|---|
| `/fix-bug <N or description>` | reproduce, classify, repair or contain one bug on its own branch |
| `/roadmap-item <R-NNNN>` | carry one roadmap task to a landable candidate |
| `/file-bug <title>` | file a new bug with `scripts/gen/new_bug.sh` |
| `/creview` | Codex review of the branch through `scripts/review/review.sh` |
| `/rebase` | the safe rebase onto `origin/main` |
| `/land` | hand a validated branch to the landing train (`scripts/land/train.sh`) |
| `/cstatus` | where this worktree, its branch, its CI and this machine stand |

`/creview` and `/cstatus` carry a `c` prefix so they do not shadow Claude Code's built-in `/review`
and `/status`; a new playbook takes a name no built-in uses.

This file owns the rules the commands share. A command links here rather than restating them, so a
rule changes in one place.

## Rules

**Design questions** are decided by [PHILOSOPHY.md](docs/PHILOSOPHY.md). Any important design decision is
put to the owner by Claude or Codex, with a recommendation reasoned from docs/PHILOSOPHY.md.

**Owner decisions: never decide one.** The language decisions OD1 – OD157
([decisions.md](docs/decisions.md#owner-decisions)) are decided only by the project owner. No
agent, orchestrator or reviewer chooses, encodes, implements or provisionally ships any side of any
of them, inside a bug fix or anywhere else. Agents present options and a recommendation to the owner
and wait for an explicit answer. Behaviour already on main that took a side stays as it is, recorded
as a conflict, until the owner answers; nobody extends or reverts it meanwhile.
- The Codex review of every fix (`scripts/review/review.sh` asks it) also answers: "does this fix
  pick a side on any OD in docs/decisions.md?" If yes, or if the fix cannot be made without picking
  one, stop and report to the owner; do not land it. Recording an answer the owner explicitly gave
  is not picking a side; what the review checks then is in
  [REVIEW.md](docs/REVIEW.md#owner-decisions-choosing-versus-recording).

**Bugs.**
- Every bug gets a structural cause and a structural owner: the design fact that let it exist, and
  the roadmap task or pipeline stage that removes the whole class
  ([STRUCTURAL_CLASSES.md](legacy/docs/bugs/STRUCTURAL_CLASSES.md)). A patch without them is not a fix.
- To file a bug, run `scripts/gen/new_bug.sh`, which writes its record and runs the ledger
  generator. To log a change, add a `changelog.d/` fragment
  ([changelog.d/README.md](legacy/changelog.d/README.md)). Never hand-edit a generated view (the bug
  lists, the class table's bugs column, the generated bug dashboard, `CHANGELOG.md`,
  `Concrete/BuildIdentity.lean`); see [scripts/tests/README.md](legacy/scripts/tests/README.md),
  "Shared ledgers".
- Each bug's compiler area is derived from its fix commits ([AREAS.md](legacy/docs/bugs/AREAS.md);
  `python3 scripts/gen/bug_ledger.py report` for the stage, class, month, silent, how-found and
  time-to-fix tables, `--json` or `--sqlite PATH` for queries). Name the bug in the subject of each
  commit that fixes it (`bug NNN: ...`), and after committing a fix rerun
  `scripts/gen/refresh_generated.sh` and commit the regenerated AREAS.md. Add an `area:` line to a
  record only when the record shows the derived area is wrong
  ([README.md](legacy/docs/bugs/README.md#bug-areas)); never fill areas in by hand otherwise.
- For `compiler: new`, a silent defect (wrong code, unsound claim) is stopped at once by repair
  or fail-closed containment; a loud one is filed, pinned and owned. Legacy-compiler defects
  are recorded against `legacy-compiler-final`, never repaired, under
  [ROADMAP's defect disposition and frozen-compiler rule](ROADMAP.md#defect-disposition-rule).

**Who works on what** (owner, 2026-11-08, [decision](docs/decisions.md#decided-2026-11-08-eleven)).
- Claude's quota goes to the critical path: the spec, slice 0 and the front end. Mechanical work
  goes to Codex and Kimi as writers.
- One writer edits `docs/decisions.md` and `ROADMAP.md` at a time; other workers send that writer
  their records instead of editing those files.

**Branches.**
- Every branch's full clone is `~/projects/clones/<branch>`, removed after the branch lands (owner,
  2026-11-08; R-0619).
- One bug or one rule per branch, in its own worktree (`scripts/worktree-new.sh`,
  [concurrent work](#concurrent-work-one-worktree-per-writer)). This applies to all agent work now, not only once
  R-0545 closes. A branch that changes a language rule lands first and stays small; work that
  depends on it waits or rebases onto it.
- Rebase onto `origin/main` after every landing (`/rebase`), not only before your own landing, so a
  conflict or a changed rule shows up while the branch is small.
- Commit each validated slice. Push the branch to `wip/<branch>` at least every 5 minutes of work
  (`git push origin HEAD:refs/heads/wip/<branch>`); the hook's wip tier only scans for conflict
  markers.
- The one repository is lambdaclass/concrete (owner, 2026-11-06), and `origin` is it in every
  clone; unbalancedparentheses/concrete2 is archived (after the first landing here).
- Never push to `main`, `ci/*` or the remote `fork`. Landings go to `origin` only through the
  landing script (`scripts/land/train.sh`).
  `train.sh check` runs the checks before the push, and the push runs the root hook's full set again;
  the train's push to `ci/<name>` and the push of `land` are read back with `ls-remote` and
  retried when they did not move the ref (GitHub drops the connection during long hooks); `land` checks that main is at
  the head ([LANDING.md](docs/LANDING.md#land)).
- After any push of your own whose hook ran gates, check that the remote ref moved
  (`git ls-remote origin refs/heads/<branch>`) and push again if it did not.

**Validation.**
- Locally, run only the impact-selected gates (pre-push runs the fast surface gates and the gates
  selected by what the push touches). For the full set, dispatch CI on the branch
  (`gh workflow run CI --ref <branch>`) or use the server (`concrete-remote wip/<branch> 'lake build
  && bash scripts/checks/run.sh'`); never a full local run on the laptop.
- Heavy legs default to the CI server (concrete-ci-2). Set `git config land.gatesHost concrete-ci-2`
  in the clone you land from, and `train.sh check` then runs its impact gates there, bound to the
  train head's SHA. The `ci` push still runs the root hook's full set; it reuses no remote result. An unreachable host is INCOMPLETE and blocks;
  run locally only with `check --local-gates` (docs/LANDING.md, "Gate host").
- At most 2 heavy runs (full suites, mutation campaigns, `lake build` of the whole tree) per machine.
  Queue behind a running one; continue light work meanwhile. Honour a user's explicit CPU limits.
- Run gates inside `nix develop`. A gate that passes by hand but fails under the hook: suspect an
  inherited `GIT_*` variable first.
- Record each result against the exact commit SHA it ran on.
- Read the obligation, not the prose: a header says what was true when it was written. Make gates
  assert properties, not counts, so an improvement does not read as a regression.

**Before asking for review.** Each of these caused an integration failure in the week to
2026-10-25; run them on the slice, in `nix develop`, before `/creview`:
- `scripts/tests/test_golden.sh` after any change to compiler output or a report.
- `scripts/tests/test_mutation.sh --check-patterns` after editing any file a mutation targets: it
  finds an anchor the edit broke in seconds, before a mutation campaign fails on it.
- `scripts/tests/check_type_identity.sh` and the other gates that elaborate Lean probes against the
  compiler (`grep -l 'lake env lean' scripts/tests/check_*.sh`) after changing a signature or a
  structure they construct.
- A fixture's `run_ok` / `run_err` leg in `run_tests.sh` spells its file name literally: the
  bug-corpus audit looks for each fixture's basename in `run_tests.sh`, so a name assembled from
  parts is reported as uncovered.
- Bound a command with `bounded_run` (`scripts/checks/lib/bounded.sh`), never GNU `timeout`: the
  macOS job has no `timeout`, and `bounded_run` reports a second timeout as INCOMPLETE, not a pass.
- A ledger `coverage:` line names only the gates that cover the bug: the filing check accepts a
  `.sh` name there as the coverage claim without running or opening it.
- File in the migrated ledger format with `scripts/gen/new_bug.sh`, which writes the `bug-ledger`
  block, a `changelog.d/` fragment and the `discovery.tsv` row (`--batch` or `--new-batch`, with
  `--silent`) and regenerates the views; after editing a record, regenerate with
  `scripts/gen/bug_ledger.py --write` and `scripts/gen/bug_dashboard.py --write`. If the script fails
  after reserving a number, rerun the `--number NNN` command it prints rather than filing by hand.
- Have Codex confirm each bug is real (a reproducer and a verdict line in the record) before
  filing it.

**Review.** Codex reviews everything before it is published or brought to the owner, and Kimi and
agy (Gemini) review every branch too; a reviewer's absence is recorded, never assumed
([decision](docs/decisions.md#decided-project-review)). agy cannot read files: run it as
`bounded_run 1500 -- agy -p "<prompt>"` with the diff and the context it needs in the prompt, ending with a
required verdict line. Every slice gets a Codex review before it lands, through `scripts/review/review.sh`:
at most 2 rounds for docs and the roadmap, 3 for scripts and 5 for the compiler and the spec
(owner, 2026-11-08; `review.sh` enforces 5 until R-0618 lands), the first over the whole diff and each later round over the delta since the
previous one. Answer each finding with a fix or a stated reason. Run Codex by hand only with
`GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` (and `</dev/null`): its sandboxed `git status` otherwise takes the
worktree's `index.lock` and can leave it behind (bug 209).

**Git safety.**
- Never pipe `git rebase` (or `git merge`, `git cherry-pick`) into anything: a pipe hides the stop.
  After every git operation, check that no merge, rebase, cherry-pick or revert is in progress
  (`git status`; `MERGE_HEAD`, `rebase-merge`, `rebase-apply` under `git rev-parse --git-dir`).
- No bare `git stash`; no interactive git (`-i`).
- Check a process's working directory (`lsof -a -p <pid> -d cwd`) before killing it; many processes
  on this machine belong to other repositories and other sessions.
- Remove a stale `index.lock` only when no process owns it (`lsof <path>/index.lock` is empty);
  otherwise wait and retry.
- Preserve other sessions' edits and untracked files. Never reset or clean a shared checkout.

**Language invariants.** Link to the normative documents instead of restating them:
[OWNERSHIP_MODEL.md](legacy/docs/archive/design-records/OWNERSHIP_MODEL.md) (linear, not affine: non-Copy values are used
exactly once), [MEMORY_SEMANTICS.md](legacy/docs/archive/design-records/MEMORY_SEMANTICS.md) (strictly second-class
references, §15), [the spec's overview](legacy/docs/spec/overview.md),
[STATEMENT_EXPRESSION_MODEL.md](legacy/docs/archive/design-records/STATEMENT_EXPRESSION_MODEL.md) (evaluation order, value
vs statement position), [KNOWN_HOLES.md](legacy/docs/verification/KNOWN_HOLES.md) (the only authority on
hole status) and [VERIFIED_PIPELINE.md](legacy/docs/compiler/VERIFIED_PIPELINE.md) (the structural owners).

## Concurrent work: one worktree per writer

**The default: every concurrent line of work gets its own git worktree.**

```sh
scripts/worktree-new.sh <name> [base-committish]
```

This is not an optimization. One working tree cannot serve two writers, because
the file system is the shared mutable state and neither writer can see the
other's intent.

### What went wrong when this was not the default

On 2026-07-31 two agents were found working in the same worktree
(`.claude/worktrees/r0442-proofcore-callee`). Every symptom was initially
misread as something else:

| symptom | what it was blamed on | what it actually was |
| --- | --- | --- |
| an edit to `ProofCore.lean` vanished, and the file dropped out of `git status` | the mutation harness | the mutation harness restoring a backup **over a concurrent edit**, then verifying against the pre-edit hash and reporting success |
| `origin/main` moved three times mid-session | stale fetches | the other agent pushing |
| two `push-both.sh` runs waiting on CI for the same SHA | a duplicated invocation | two agents each publishing |

The first row is the expensive one: work was destroyed *and* the tool reported
that nothing was wrong. A postcondition that compares against a stale baseline
confirms the corruption it was meant to catch.

### What a worktree does and does not isolate

| | |
| --- | --- |
| **Isolated** | working tree, index, `HEAD`, current branch |
| **Shared** | object store, remotes, **the stash stack**, branch namespace |

So worktrees fix file-level races, not coordination:

- Pushes must still be sequenced — hence the lock in `push-both.sh`.
- `git stash` is still unsafe across worktrees; bare `git stash` / `git stash pop`
  is banned repo-wide. Use a temporary WIP commit, or
  `git stash push -u -m "<unique-tag>"` and recover by SHA with `apply`, never
  `pop`.
- A branch name is a shared resource. `worktree-new.sh` refuses to reuse one
  rather than let `git` fail halfway, because the existing branch may belong to
  another writer.

### Enforcement is at the point of danger, not by head-count

Two heuristics for "is another agent here?" were measured and both cried wolf:

- matching process names (`pgrep -lf "codex|claude"`) reported **115** — every
  shell wrapper's command line contains the `.claude` snapshot path;
- counting processes whose CWD is the tree reported **7** in a worktree freshly
  created by a single agent — its own shells and background waiters all sit there.

Neither distinguishes "two writers" from "one agent working normally", and a
warning that fires on non-events trains the reader to ignore the one that
matters. So concurrency is caught where the signal is a fact:

- **`scripts/tests/test_mutation.sh`** records a hash of the content it itself
  wrote. At restore time, content that is neither its mutation nor the original
  means a third party wrote the file: it **refuses to restore**, preserves their
  version under `CONCURRENT-EDIT/`, keeps its own backup for reconciliation, and
  fails the run. It cannot silently overwrite an edit it did not make.
- **`scripts/push-both.sh`** takes an exclusive lock in the **common** git dir
  (`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`), not the worktree — remotes are shared, so a
  per-worktree lock would miss two worktrees publishing at once.

### Working while something long is running

Long-running work does not always require an idle tree. Distinguish the phases:

| phase | tree must be stable? |
| --- | --- |
| pre-push hook running gates | **yes** — gates read the working tree |
| `run_ci_gates_local.sh` | **yes** — same reason |
| `test_mutation.sh` | **yes** — it rewrites source in place |
| `push-both.sh` waiting for remote CI | **no** — it only polls `gh` |

The CI wait touches no files, so work can continue during it — in another
worktree. Treating the whole publish as tree-freezing is over-caution; treating
the hook phase as safe is the error that costs a run.

### Landing, review and load (development throughput)

On 2026-10-22 a finished branch took 6 – 10 h to reach main for about 1 h of work.
The [development throughput](ROADMAP.md#development-throughput) track in the
roadmap owns the repair. These rules apply to every writer now:

- **One bug or one rule per branch** (R-0545). A branch that changes a language or
  checker rule lands first and small, with the migrations of main's fixtures and
  nothing else. A branch that needs two rules is two branches.
- **Review per slice, delta after the first pass** (R-0544). After the first full
  review, each round reviews only the diff since the previous round. Past the risk
  cap (the round caps under **Review** above),
  split the slice. Review ownership and memory-safety work with neutral prompts and
  piecewise diffs.
- **At most 2 heavy local runs per machine** (R-0543). Full gate sets, closure-mode
  runs and mutation campaigns count as heavy. Agents run the fast and
  impact-selected gates; full sets go to the servers (R-0541) or to CI. The
  machine-wide semaphore `scripts/checks/lib/heavy_slots.sh` holds the full runners,
  `run_tests.sh --full` and the mutation drivers to two slots; for any other heavy
  command, check the load and queue behind a running heavy run instead of starting a third.
- **Ready branches land together** (R-0539). Ready branches are integrated into one
  train candidate with one CI run, rather than each rebasing onto the last landing,
  through `scripts/land/train.sh` ([LANDING.md](docs/LANDING.md)). A train built by hand
  follows the same rules: one integrated candidate, results recorded against its
  exact SHA, and bisection by member only on red.

### Running the full gate set on the server

The full set runs on a server (or in CI), never on the laptop:

```
~/bin/concrete-remote <pushed-ref> \
  'lake build && bash scripts/checks/run.sh'
```

`concrete-remote` checks the ref out as a `git clone --shared` (not a worktree:
two mutation-driver gates refuse worktrees) and sets the runner's default width
to the measured best for that host (`CONCRETE_GATE_JOBS_DEFAULT`; override with
`CONCRETE_REMOTE_JOBS=N`, or pass `JOBS=N` in the command). The measurements and
the critical path were taken on concrete-ci-1, since deleted (2026-10-23), and are
in [GATE_PROFILE.md](legacy/docs/project/GATE_PROFILE.md#parallel-scaling-on-concrete-ci-1-r-0546-step-4).

**A gate must be safe to run beside every other gate.** In a pass the gates run
JOBS-wide in one checkout, and several passes run on one host. So a gate:

- writes scratch only under `mktemp -d` (or a `.$$` name), never a fixed
  `/tmp/<name>` — `check_gate_hygiene.sh` refuses fixed `/tmp` paths;
- never edits a tracked file in place, not even to restore it a moment later.
  A probe that perturbs a source runs in a private copy of the checkout
  (`run_fast_surface_gates.sh --mutate`, `check_attestation_manifest.sh`). An
  in-place edit of a `.lean` file makes every concurrent `require_fresh_binary`
  rebuild the shared compiler from it;
- runs sub-steps concurrently only under one lock it already holds (fresh.sh
  refuses a second concurrent acquisition) and records each sub-step's exit
  status, so a lost result is a failure, not a pass.

A pool failure that passes on the runner's sequential re-check is listed under
"Recovered under sequential re-run" and in `.ci-gate-timings.tsv` (stage
`pool`, verdict `FAIL`, then stage `recheck`). Treat it as a race in that gate
to fix, not as noise.

### Killing a publish does not un-publish it

`push-both.sh` runs `git push` as a CHILD process. Killing the script does not
kill that child, and a `git push` already in flight will complete.

Measured 2026-07-31: a publish was killed during its pre-push hook, `git ls-remote`
showed the primary unchanged, and that was read as "nothing was published". The
`git push` child was still running; it finished moments later and advanced the
primary. The next publish was then rejected as non-fast-forward — harmless here,
but the reasoning behind the kill was a race read as a guarantee.

So: a ref check taken *at* the moment of the kill proves nothing about a push
that has already started. If a publish must be abandoned, re-check the remote
AFTER the process tree is gone (`pgrep -f "git push"` clear), and treat the
remote's actual state as the answer rather than the state you saw before killing.

### Orchestrating a publish: never infer state from process names

`pgrep -f "push-both"` matches the WAITER'S OWN command line, which contains that
string. Every `until ! pgrep -f "push-both"; do sleep 30; done` loop therefore
waits on itself forever, and a status check written the same way reports "publish
running" when nothing is. Measured 2026-08-01: six background tasks were stuck on
this at once, and the reported state was wrong for as long as they ran. It is the
same failure as counting `pgrep -lf "codex|claude"` and getting 115 — a
process-name match catches the matcher.

Do this instead:

* capture the launched PID and `wait "$pid"`;
* verify completion by checking the EXACT REMOTE SHA (`git ls-remote`), which is
  the fact you actually care about;
* query CI for that SHA (`gh run list --commit <full-sha>` — the full SHA; the
  short form returns nothing);
* never infer state from a process-name match.

For a one-off inspection, `ps -eo command | grep -c "[p]ush-both.sh"` excludes
itself via the bracket, but a ref check is still the better answer.

### Reviewing a branch

Every agent reviews the same way: `scripts/review/review.sh`, one round per
invocation, with the verdict recorded as a git note on the reviewed commit. The
note is shared through `origin`, so rounds run in another worktree or by another
agent count toward the same cap, and the next round reviews only the delta. See
[REVIEW.md](docs/REVIEW.md).
# The Concrete Programming Language

[![CI](https://github.com/lambdaclass/concrete/actions/workflows/lean_action_ci.yml/badge.svg)](https://github.com/lambdaclass/concrete/actions/workflows/lean_action_ci.yml)
[![license](https://img.shields.io/github/license/lambdaclass/concrete)](LICENSE)

**Concrete is a fully proven systems programming language with no garbage collector, where you can
know everything a program does: what it does, what it may touch, whether it can fail or stop, and
what is proved about it.** Every unproven step is named as an explicit assumption, never silently
trusted. This states the project's goal: each guarantee holds only as its evidence lands, compiler
correctness and program correctness need separate proofs, and native claims remain conditional on
their named assumptions ([decision](docs/decisions.md#decided-project-core-statement)).

It checks resource ownership, makes external authority explicit at every use, and is specified in
Lean so that its rules can be proved about. From the first compiler slice, you can ask the compiler
what any function may do: its capabilities and reachable resources, allocation, trusted and foreign
boundaries, how each possible failure is handled, termination and proof status, each with its reason
and evidence ([decision](docs/decisions.md#decided-project-facts-report)).

Accepted safe code has no hidden traps: an index, a division, a shift or an arithmetic operation
is either proven safe when the program is compiled or has its failure handled in the code. Before a
program runs, every function has termination evidence with its premises, or a report naming the
explicitly permitted unresolved obligations, each traced to a written permission at the loop or
boundary responsible, and the compiler reports recursion depth, stack use, allocation,
blocking and foreign calls per function, SPARK-style. Each guarantee holds as its evidence lands
([decision](docs/decisions.md#decided-project-no-hidden-traps)).

**Status: restarting.** A new compiler is being built from a Lean specification. Nothing at this
root compiles Concrete programs yet.

## Where to read

- **Why Concrete exists and the rules it follows:** [docs/PHILOSOPHY.md](docs/PHILOSOPHY.md).
- **The language (normative):** [ConcreteSpec/](ConcreteSpec/). Its README, the reading guide, and
  the browsable owner view generated by `scripts/spec/lean_site.sh` land with the integrated spec
  (A2i).
- **The compiler architecture:** `Concrete/README.md`, which lands with slice 0
  ([the pipeline decision](docs/decisions.md#decided-project-pipeline-2026-11-06) summarizes it).
- **Decisions, and what waits for the owner:** [docs/decisions.md](docs/decisions.md).
- **Next work:** [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md).
- **Proposals awaiting the owner:** [docs/proposals/](docs/proposals/).

A non-normative tutorial comes in phase C (R-0334).

| [ISSUES.tsv](ISSUES.tsv) | Defects in the spec, proofs, CI and tooling, until the new bug ledger returns. |
| [RETENTION.tsv](RETENTION.tsv) | Where every asset of the old tree went, and when it may be deleted. |
| [legacy/](legacy/) | The old compiler and everything around it, frozen byte for byte. |

## What Concrete learns from

| Part of Concrete | Main precedent |
|---|---|
| Linear ownership, scoped borrows, capabilities in signatures | Austral |
| Mutable value semantics: mutation through scoped access, no returned or stored references | Hylo (formerly Val) |
| A small explicit language: no hidden control flow or allocation, allocators passed as parameters | Zig |
| Compiler-enforced safety culture and an explicit trusted boundary | Rust |
| Verified compilation: source behaviour carried down to the binary | CakeML and CompCert |
| Absence of run-time errors and the assurance workflow | SPARK, with Dafny and Why3 |
| Rules and proofs in one checked system; the implementation language | Lean 4 |

Smaller influences include Aeneas and Effekt. The full comparison, including what Concrete takes
from each and what it rejects, is archived in
[legacy/docs/project/INFLUENCES.md](legacy/docs/project/INFLUENCES.md).

## The old compiler

`legacy/` is a frozen snapshot of the old tree: it is never edited and never run here. The old
compiler is tag `legacy-compiler-final`; to use it, check that tag out in its own worktree. The
differential oracle is built from the tag, not from `legacy/` (`scripts/oracle/`). Assets the new
compiler still needs (programs, examples, std, the bug ledger, the site) stay archived in `legacy/`
until they migrate; [RETENTION.tsv](RETENTION.tsv) records each one.

## Building

```sh
nix develop            # Lean and the tools the checks use
lake build             # the specification
bash scripts/checks/run.sh   # the root checks (also run by the pre-push hook and CI)
```
