  [ "$rounds" -ge "$cap" ] \
    || bad "the review cap was not reached: a $cls change has cap $cap, and ${at:0:10} records $rounds round(s)"
  local r n entry file hash got reviewed verdict
  for r in $REVIEWERS; do
    n="F_request_$r"; [ -n "${!n}" ] || bad "the record has no $r request"
    n="F_verdict_$r"; [ -n "${!n}" ] || bad "the record has no $r verdict"
  done
  local want="100644 blob agy-request.txt 100644 blob agy.txt 100644 blob codex-request.txt 100644 blob codex.txt 100644 blob kimi-request.txt 100644 blob kimi.txt 100644 blob record.txt " have
  have="$(git ls-tree --format='%(objectmode) %(objecttype) %(path)' "$obj" | LC_ALL=C sort | tr '\n' ' ')"
  [ "$have" = "$want" ] || bad "the record tree is not exactly the seven sign-off files as 100644 blobs"
  for r in $REVIEWERS; do
    n="F_request_$r"; entry="${!n}"
    file="${entry%% *}" hash="${entry#* }"
    [ "$file" = "$r-request.txt" ] && [[ "$hash" =~ ^[0-9a-f]{64}$ ]] || bad "request-$r is '$entry', not '$r-request.txt <sha256>'"
    got="$(git cat-file blob "$obj:$file" | sha256)"
    [ "$got" = "$hash" ] || bad "the sha256 of $file is $got, not the recorded $hash"
    reviewed="$(git cat-file blob "$obj:$file" | sed -n '1p')"
    [[ "$reviewed" =~ ^reviewed:\ [0-9a-f]{40}$ ]] || bad "the $r request's first line is not a 'reviewed: <full 40-hex SHA>' line"
    [ "$reviewed" = "reviewed: $sha" ] || bad "the $r request's reviewed line is '${reviewed#reviewed: }', not $sha"
    git cat-file blob "$obj:$file" | tail -n +2 | names "$sha" \
      || bad "the $r request body does not name ${sha:0:10} (an unambiguous prefix of at least 7 hex digits)"
    n="F_verdict_$r"; entry="${!n}"
    file="${entry%% *}" hash="${entry#* }"
    [ "$file" = "$r.txt" ] && [[ "$hash" =~ ^[0-9a-f]{64}$ ]] || bad "verdict-$r is '$entry', not '$r.txt <sha256>'"
    got="$(git cat-file blob "$obj:$file" | sha256)"
    [ "$got" = "$hash" ] || bad "the sha256 of $file is $got, not the recorded $hash"
    verdict="$(git cat-file blob "$obj:$file" | grep -E '^[[:space:]]*VERDICT: (ACCEPT|REJECT)[[:space:]]*$' | tail -1 | tr -d '[:space:]')" || true
    [ -n "$verdict" ] || bad "the $r output has no verdict"
    [ "$verdict" = "VERDICT:ACCEPT" ] || bad "the $r output's last verdict is '$verdict', not VERDICT: ACCEPT"
  done
  echo "$sha signed off by the owner ($F_date; $cls change, cap $cap reached at ${at:0:10} with $rounds round(s); codex, kimi and agy accepted): \"$F_quote\""
}

cmd_verify() {
  local sha base=""
  [ $# -ge 1 ] || die "usage: signoff.sh verify <commit> --base BASE"
  sha="$(git rev-parse -q --verify "$1^{commit}")" || die "verify: not a commit: $1"; shift
  while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do
    case "$1" in --base) [ $# -ge 2 ] || die "verify: --base needs a value"; base="$2"; shift 2 ;; *) die "verify: unknown option $1" ;; esac
  done
  [ -n "$base" ] || die "verify: --base BASE is required (the cap is the class of the diff against it)"
  base="$(git rev-parse -q --verify "$base^{commit}")" || die "verify: --base is not a commit"
  git rev-parse -q --verify "$PREFIX/$sha^{commit}" >/dev/null || { echo "$sha has no owner sign-off ($PREFIX/$sha)"; exit 2; }
  check "$sha" "$(git rev-parse "$PREFIX/$sha^{commit}")" "$base"
}

case "${1:-}" in
  record) shift; cmd_record "$@" ;;
  verify) shift; cmd_verify "$@" ;;
  -h|--help) usage ;;
  *) usage >&2; exit 2 ;;
esac

exec
/bin/bash -lc 'cat scripts/land/acceptance.sh scripts/oracle/ORACLE.lock scripts/oracle/build.sh scripts/push-both.sh' in /tmp/claude-30035/-home-unbalancedparen-projects-concrete2/e2c54e65-31b5-4145-8101-c2d7b071b97e/scratchpad/codexfull/119381451a570461935b186ec3f62ac96b020125/tree
 succeeded in 0ms:
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# THE LANDING WORKFLOW ACCEPTANCE TEST (ROADMAP R-0563 on the new roadmap): does a fresh clone still build, hook, run CI
# at the exact SHA and get as far as a landing, and do the refusals still refuse?
#
# Run it, in the clone that will land, for every candidate train.sh's move gate selects (a change
# under legacy/, or to the root inventory's top-level entries; see is_move_candidate). It drives the
# real scripts/land/train.sh from a FRESH clone, so nothing in this checkout, its state directory or
# its hooks configuration can make it pass.
#
# IT NEVER LANDS, and that does not rest on the script under test honouring --dry-run. Three
# independent guards: a `git` shim first on PATH for every leg refuses any push whose destination is
# main (or --all, --mirror, a bare HEAD), whatever flags the candidate's scripts pass, --no-verify
# included; the clone's hooks path is a pre-push guard that refuses the same before it runs the real
# hook; and main is read before and after, a move to the train head being a failure.
# The train head's publishing scripts are also refused if they run `git push ... --no-verify`. It refuses to start with any LAND_* or CONCRETE_SKIP_* variable set, since those
# redirect remotes, swap the hook or skip the gates.
#
#   acceptance.sh --ref REF [--name NAME] [--skip LEG]... [--run-id ID] [--keep]
#
#   --ref REF      what to accept, as a train member: a ref not yet on main (a wip/ branch); required,
#                  because train.sh refuses a member main already contains
#   --name NAME    train name (default accept-<short sha of REF>)
#   --skip LEG     skip one leg; a skipped leg makes the verdict `incomplete`, never `accepted`
#   --run-id ID    reuse a completed CI run of this exact train head instead of dispatching one
#   --keep         keep the clone
#
# Legs, in order (each stops the run when it fails, except the negative controls, which all run):
#   clone    git clone of origin, the guarded hooks path installed
#   train    train.sh build <name> <ref>; the train head is the SHA every later result is bound to
#   build    ACCEPT_BUILD_CMD in the clone, at the train head (default: nix develop --command lake
#            build, the root's build)
#   check    train.sh check <name> --no-remote: the impact selection over base..head, and the review
#            leg, which skips nothing: it passes on verified clean review notes (the train head's, or
#            every member's at its exact SHA), else runs the reviewer, and fails closed. The remote
#            full set is left to the ci and watch legs, which run every required job at the exact SHA
#   ci       train.sh ci <name>: push ci/<name>, dispatch CI, record the run of the exact head
#   watch    train.sh watch <name>: the run's head SHA, landing tier and required-checks.txt
#   dryland  train.sh --dry-run land <name>: every land check, nothing pushed
#   negative controls, each of which must FAIL, for its own reason:
#     neg:build  a commit on the train head that breaks the build (ACCEPT_BREAK_BUILD, a shell
#                snippet run in a scratch clone): the build leg's command must fail, naming the file
#     neg:hook   a commit on the train head that adds a job name to required-checks.txt that no
#                workflow has: the candidate's own pre-push hook, fed that range, must fail
#     neg:<m>    the same watch and dry-run land against the same run with its verdict tampered (a
#                gh wrapper edits the JSON): a required job removed (drop), skipped, cancelled or
#                failed, and the run's head SHA changed (sha). Each must be refused by BOTH commands
#                with that mode's own diagnostic, and the wrapper must record that it changed the
#                verdict; a refusal for any other reason (gh failing, a moved main) fails the control.
#   Before the tampered controls, the real run is read once more: its head SHA must be the train head.
#
# Output: one line per leg (passed | failed | skipped | incomplete) and a verdict: accepted (every
# leg passed), incomplete (none failed, some skipped, or main moved before the dry-run land so
# train.sh refused the stale candidate; re-run), or failed. Results are bound to the train's head SHA and
# appended to <state>/acceptance.tsv; the verdict is also appended to <state>/rehearsals.tsv, keyed by
# the train head's tree and base, which is the record train.sh's move gate requires before the legacy/
# move lands (<state> = <git common dir>/concrete-landing of the clone this runs in: run it in the
# clone that will land). Exit 0 only for `accepted`.
set -uo pipefail

SELF_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
# shellcheck source=scripts/land/lib.sh
. "$SELF_DIR/lib.sh"
# The clone and scratch repositories below are sandboxes: none of the caller's repository variables
# may reach them (ISS-0015). The clone is found from the working directory from here on.
. "$SELF_DIR/../checks/lib/git_sandbox.sh"
git_sandbox || exit 2
REF="" NAME="" RUN_ID="" KEEP=0
declare -A SKIP=()
while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do
  case "$1" in
    --ref) REF="$2"; shift ;;
    --name) NAME="$2"; shift ;;
    --skip) SKIP["$2"]=1; shift ;;
    --run-id) RUN_ID="$2"; shift ;;
    --keep) KEEP=1 ;;
    -h|--help) awk 'NR > 1 { if (!/^#/) exit; sub(/^# ?/, ""); print }' "$0"; exit 0 ;;
    *) echo "acceptance: unknown argument $1" >&2; exit 2 ;;
  esac; shift
done
[ -n "$REF" ] || { echo "acceptance: --ref is required (a ref not yet on main)" >&2; exit 2; }
# REF and NAME go into the tab-separated records (rehearsals.tsv is what train.sh's move gate trusts),
# so they are checked before any leg runs or any row is written: a refused run writes nothing.
case "$REF" in *[[:space:]]*|*[[:cntrl:]]*) echo "acceptance: --ref must not contain whitespace or control characters" >&2; exit 2 ;; esac
case "$NAME" in *[!A-Za-z0-9._-]*|.*|*..*) echo "acceptance: --name must be [A-Za-z0-9._-]+ and not start with '.'" >&2; exit 2 ;; esac
bad_env="$(compgen -e | grep -E '^(LAND_|CONCRETE_SKIP_)' || true)"
[ -z "$bad_env" ] || { echo "acceptance: refusing to run with $(echo $bad_env) set (remote, hook or gate overrides)" >&2; exit 2; }

# The one build command of the build leg and of the broken-build control (neg:build).
BUILD_CMD="${ACCEPT_BUILD_CMD:-nix develop --command lake build}"

SRC_REPO="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null)" || { echo "acceptance: run inside a clone" >&2; exit 2; }
ORIGIN_URL="$(git -C "$SRC_REPO" remote get-url origin)"
WORK="$(mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/acceptance.XXXXXX")"
[ "$KEEP" = 1 ] || trap 'rm -rf "$WORK"' EXIT
C="$WORK/clone"
declare -a ROWS=()
FAILED=0 SKIPPED=0 HEAD=""
MAIN_BEFORE=""
row() { ROWS+=("$1"$'\t'"$2"$'\t'"$3"); printf '  %-8s %-8s %s\n' "$1" "$2" "$3"; }
leg() { # name cmd... — runs unless skipped; a failure ends the run (the negative leg has its own loop)
  local n="$1"; shift
  if [ -n "${SKIP[$n]:-}" ]; then row "$n" skipped "--skip"; SKIPPED=1; return 0; fi
  local log="$WORK/$n.log"
  if "$@" > "$log" 2>&1; then row "$n" passed "$(tail -n 1 "$log" | cut -c1-100)"; return 0; fi
  if [ "$n" = dryland ] && grep -q "origin/main moved from" "$log"; then
    # Another landing moved main under the run: train.sh refused correctly, but the run proves
    # nothing about this candidate's dry-run land or the tamper controls. Incomplete, never passed.
    row "$n" incomplete "main moved during the run; train.sh refused the stale candidate (see $log)"; SKIPPED=1; finish
  fi
  row "$n" failed "see $log"; tail -n 30 "$log" | sed 's/^/      /'; FAILED=1; finish
}
main_heads() { git -C "$C" ls-remote origin refs/heads/main 2>/dev/null | cut -f1 | tr '\n' ' '; }
finish() {
  if [ -n "$MAIN_BEFORE" ]; then
    local after; after="$(main_heads)"
    if [ "$after" != "$MAIN_BEFORE" ]; then
      case " $after " in
        *" ${HEAD:-none} "*) row main failed "main moved to the train head: the test LANDED"; FAILED=1 ;;
        *) row main note "main moved during the run (not to the train head): $MAIN_BEFORE-> $after" ;;
      esac
    fi
  fi
  local verdict=accepted
  [ "$SKIPPED" = 1 ] && verdict=incomplete
  [ "$FAILED" = 1 ] && verdict=failed
  local state; state="$(cd "$SRC_REPO" && cd "$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)" && pwd)/concrete-landing"
  local rec="${ACCEPT_RECORD:-$state/acceptance.tsv}"
  mkdir -p "$(dirname "$rec")"
  for r in "${ROWS[@]}"; do printf '%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\n' "$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" "${HEAD:-?}" "$NAME" "$r"; done >> "$rec"
  printf '%s\t%s\t%s\tverdict\t%s\t\n' "$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" "${HEAD:-?}" "$NAME" "$verdict" >> "$rec"
  # The rehearsal record train.sh's move gate reads (require_rehearsal): one row per run that built a
  # train, keyed by the train head's tree and base, so the landing train (a new merge commit of the
  # same base and members) finds it. Written for every verdict; only `accepted` satisfies the gate. A
  # run that failed before it had a train head, tree and base (a bad ref, a failed build) binds no
  # candidate and writes no row there (acceptance.tsv above still records it): train.sh refuses a
  # malformed row outright, so an unbound row would block every later land.
  local reh="${ACCEPT_REHEARSALS:-$state/rehearsals.tsv}" tree="" base=""
  if [ -n "$HEAD" ]; then
    tree="$(git -C "$C" rev-parse "$HEAD^{tree}" 2>/dev/null || true)"
    base="$(sed -n 's/^base=//p' "$(cd "$C" && cd "$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)" && pwd)/concrete-landing/$NAME/train.env" 2>/dev/null | tail -n 1)"
  fi
  if [[ "$HEAD" =~ ^[0-9a-f]{40}$ && "$tree" =~ ^[0-9a-f]{40}$ && "$base" =~ ^[0-9a-f]{40}$ ]]; then
    mkdir -p "$(dirname "$reh")"
    [ -s "$reh" ] || printf '# utc\tverdict\ttrain_head\ttrain_head_tree\tbase\tref\tname (scripts/land/acceptance.sh; read by train.sh require_rehearsal)\n' > "$reh"
    printf '%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\n' "$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" "$verdict" "$HEAD" "$tree" "$base" "$REF" "$NAME" >> "$reh"
  else
    echo "acceptance: no train head, tree and base, so no rehearsal row (this run binds no candidate)"
  fi
  local kept=""; [ "$KEEP" = 1 ] && kept="; clone $C"
  echo "acceptance: $verdict at ${HEAD:-?} (train $NAME; record $rec$kept)"
  [ "$verdict" = accepted ]; exit $?
}

TRAIN() { ( cd "$C" && bash scripts/land/train.sh "$@" ); }

# The git shim: every leg runs with it first on PATH. It refuses a push that could move main, and
# passes everything else to the real git.
make_git_shim() {
  local real; real="$(command -v git)"
  mkdir -p "$WORK/bin"
  cat > "$WORK/bin/git" <<SHIM
#!/usr/bin/env bash
seen=0
for a in "\$@"; do
  if [ "\$seen" = 0 ]; then [ "\$a" = push ] && seen=1; continue; fi
  case "\$a" in
    --all|--mirror|HEAD|main|refs/heads/main|*:main|*:refs/heads/main|+*:main|+*:refs/heads/main)
      echo "acceptance git shim: refusing push argument '\$a' (could move main)" >&2; exit 1 ;;
  esac
done
exec "$real" "\$@"
SHIM
  chmod +x "$WORK/bin/git"
}
# The guard hooks path: pre-push refuses any main update, then runs the real hook on the same input.
make_guard() {
  mkdir -p "$WORK/guard-hooks"
  cat > "$WORK/guard-hooks/pre-push" <<GUARD
#!/usr/bin/env bash
in="\$(cat)"
while read -r _l _ls rref _rs; do
  case "\$rref" in refs/heads/main|main) echo "acceptance guard: refusing a push to \$rref on \$1" >&2; exit 1 ;; esac
done <<< "\$in"
[ -x "$C/.githooks/pre-push" ] || [ -f "$C/.githooks/pre-push" ] || exit 0
printf '%s\n' "\$in" | bash "$C/.githooks/pre-push" "\$@"
GUARD
  chmod +x "$WORK/guard-hooks/pre-push"
}
do_clone() {
  git clone --quiet "$ORIGIN_URL" "$C" && cd "$C" || return 1
  make_guard
  git config core.hooksPath "$WORK/guard-hooks"
  MAIN_BEFORE="$(main_heads)"
  git fetch --quiet origin '+refs/heads/wip/*:refs/remotes/origin/wip/*' || true
  git rev-parse --verify --quiet "$REF^{commit}" >/dev/null || { echo "no ref $REF in the fresh clone"; return 1; }
  [ -f scripts/land/train.sh ] || { echo "no scripts/land/train.sh"; return 1; }
  echo "cloned $ORIGIN_URL; $REF = $(git rev-parse "$REF")"
}
do_build() { # at the train head: the build result belongs to the SHA every other leg tests
  [ "$(git -C "$C" rev-parse HEAD)" = "$HEAD" ] || { echo "the clone is not at the train head"; return 1; }
  cd "$C" && bash -c "$BUILD_CMD" && echo "built $HEAD"
}
do_train() {
  TRAIN build "$NAME" "$REF" && HEAD="$(git -C "$C" rev-parse HEAD)" || return 1
  # The candidate's own publishing scripts, at the train head, may not skip hooks on a push.
  local hits; hits="$(cd "$C" && grep -rnE -- 'git[^#]*push[^#]*--no-verify|--no-verify[^#]*push' scripts/land scripts/push-both.sh 2>/dev/null \
    | grep -v '^scripts/land/acceptance.sh:' | grep -vE '^[^:]+:[0-9]+:[[:space:]]*#' || true)"
  [ -z "$hits" ] || { echo "the train head's landing scripts push with --no-verify:"; echo "$hits"; return 1; }
  echo "train head $HEAD"
}
do_check() { TRAIN check "$NAME" --no-remote; }
do_ci() {
  if [ -n "$RUN_ID" ]; then
    local f; f="$(cd "$C" && cd "$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)" && pwd)/concrete-landing/$NAME/train.env"
    grep -v '^run_id=' "$f" > "$f.tmp"; echo "run_id=$RUN_ID" >> "$f.tmp"; mv "$f.tmp" "$f"
    echo "reusing run $RUN_ID (watch checks that it ran $HEAD)"
  else
    TRAIN ci "$NAME"
  fi
}
do_watch() { TRAIN watch "$NAME" --interval 60; }
# A move candidate (R-0558) cannot pass the dry-run land outright: train.sh's move gate wants the
# accepted rehearsal this very run is producing. train.sh puts that gate after every other land check
# and exits 5 when it is the only refusal, so 5 with that diagnostic is this leg passing; any other
# refusal, and a 5 without it, is not.
do_dryland() {
  local out rc
  out="$(TRAIN --dry-run land "$NAME" 2>&1)"; rc=$?
  printf '%s\n' "$out"
  [ "$rc" = 0 ] && return 0
  if dryland_only_move_gate "$rc" "$out"; then
    echo "dry-run land: every land check passed except the move gate, which this run's record satisfies"
    return 0
  fi
  return "$rc"
}

# The tampering gh: real `gh`, except that `run view --json ...` output is edited per $ACCEPT_TAMPER.
# Each edit it actually makes is appended to $ACCEPT_TAMPER_LOG, so a control can prove its mutation.
make_tamper_gh() {
  cat > "$WORK/tamper-gh" <<'GH'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
if [ "$1 $2" != "run view" ]; then exec gh "$@"; fi
gh "$@" | python3 -I -c '
import json, sys, os
mode = os.environ["ACCEPT_TAMPER"]; req = os.environ["ACCEPT_REQ"]
d = json.load(sys.stdin)
jobs = d.get("jobs")
done = []
if mode == "sha" and "headSha" in d:
    d["headSha"] = "0" * 40; done.append("headSha")
if jobs is not None:
    if mode == "drop":
        d["jobs"] = [j for j in jobs if j.get("name") != req]
        if len(d["jobs"]) != len(jobs): done.append("dropped " + req)
    elif mode in ("skipped", "cancelled", "failure"):
        for j in jobs:
            if j.get("name") == req and j.get("conclusion") != mode: j["conclusion"] = mode; done.append(mode + " " + req)
if done:
    open(os.environ["ACCEPT_TAMPER_LOG"], "a").write(mode + ": " + ", ".join(done) + "\n")
json.dump(d, sys.stdout)'
GH
  chmod +x "$WORK/tamper-gh"
}
# A scratch clone at the train head with one breaking commit; prints its path, or nothing. A clone,
# not a worktree: the pre-push hook refuses a gated push from a linked worktree (ISS-0015), so a
# worktree would make neg:hook fail for that reason instead of the broken commit.
neg_tree() { # name snippet
  local d="$WORK/neg-$1"
  { git clone -q --local --no-checkout "$C" "$d" && git -C "$d" checkout -q --detach "$HEAD"; } >/dev/null 2>&1 || return 1
  ( cd "$d" && bash -c "$2" && git add -A && git -c user.name=acceptance -c user.email=acceptance@invalid commit -qm "acceptance: broken on purpose ($1)" ) >/dev/null 2>&1 || return 1
  echo "$d"
}
do_neg_build() {
  if [ -n "${SKIP[build]:-}" ]; then row neg:build skipped "the build leg is skipped"; SKIPPED=1; return 0; fi
  local snip="${ACCEPT_BREAK_BUILD:-f=ConcreteSpec.lean; [ -f \$f ] || f=Main.lean; printf '\ntheorem acceptance_broken : False := rfl\n' >> \$f; echo \$f > .acceptance-broken-file}"
  local d out f
  d="$(neg_tree build "$snip")" || { row neg:build failed "could not make the broken commit"; FAILED=1; return 0; }
  f="$(git -C "$d" show HEAD:.acceptance-broken-file 2>/dev/null)"
  out="$(cd "$d" && bash -c "$BUILD_CMD" 2>&1)"
  if [ $? = 0 ]; then row neg:build failed "a broken build passed"; FAILED=1
  elif [ -n "$f" ] && ! printf '%s\n' "$out" | awk -v w="$f" 'index($0, w) {x=1} END {exit !x}'; then
    row neg:build failed "the build failed, but not on the broken file $f"; FAILED=1
  else row neg:build passed "build refused the broken commit${f:+ ($f)}"; fi
  rm -rf "$d"
}
do_neg_hook() {
  local d out base
  d="$(neg_tree hook "printf 'Acceptance: no such job\n' >> required-checks.txt")" || { row neg:hook failed "could not make the broken commit"; FAILED=1; return 0; }
  [ -f "$d/.githooks/pre-push" ] || { row neg:hook failed "the candidate has no .githooks/pre-push"; FAILED=1; return 0; }
  base="$(git -C "$C" rev-parse "$HEAD")"
  out="$(cd "$d" && printf 'refs/heads/acceptance-neg %s refs/heads/ci/acceptance-neg %s\n' "$(git rev-parse HEAD)" "$base" \
         | bash .githooks/pre-push origin "$ORIGIN_URL" 2>&1)"
  if [ $? = 0 ]; then row neg:hook failed "the hook passed a required-checks.txt naming a job no workflow has"; FAILED=1
  elif ! printf '%s\n' "$out" | awk 'index($0, "landing_script") {x=1} END {exit !x}'; then
    row neg:hook failed "the hook failed, but not in the landing-script check: $(printf '%s\n' "$out" | tail -n 1 | cut -c1-80)"; FAILED=1
  else row neg:hook passed "the hook refused (landing-script check)"; fi
  rm -rf "$d"
}
do_negative() {
  if [ -n "${SKIP[negative]:-}" ]; then row negative skipped "--skip"; SKIPPED=1; return 0; fi
  local rid rsha
  rid="$(cd "$C" && sed -n 's/^run_id=//p' "$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)/concrete-landing/$NAME/train.env" | tail -1)"
  rsha="$(gh run view "$rid" -R "${ACCEPT_REPO:-lambdaclass/concrete}" --json headSha 2>/dev/null | python3 -I -c 'import json,sys; print(json.load(sys.stdin).get("headSha",""))' 2>/dev/null)"
  if [ "$rsha" != "$HEAD" ]; then row neg:run failed "run ${rid:-?} is at ${rsha:-?}, not the train head $HEAD: the controls would test another SHA"; FAILED=1; return 0; fi
  row neg:run passed "run $rid is at the train head ${HEAD:0:10}"
  make_tamper_gh
  local req; req="$(sed 's/#.*//; s/^[[:space:]]*//; s/[[:space:]]*$//' "$C/required-checks.txt" 2>/dev/null | sed '/^$/d' | head -1)"
  if [ -z "$req" ]; then row negative failed "no required-checks.txt in the candidate"; FAILED=1; return 0; fi
  local mode cmd out rc want tlog
  for mode in drop skipped cancelled failure sha; do
    for cmd in watch land; do
      tlog="$WORK/tamper-$mode-$cmd.log"; : > "$tlog"
      case "$mode:$cmd" in
        drop:*) want="missing: $req" ;;
        skipped:*) want="skipped: $req" ;;
        cancelled:*) want="cancelled: $req" ;;
        failure:*) want="failure: $req" ;;
        sha:watch) want="says nothing about this train" ;;
        sha:land) want="not the train HEAD" ;;
      esac
      if [ "$cmd" = watch ]; then
        out="$(cd "$C" && ACCEPT_TAMPER=$mode ACCEPT_REQ="$req" ACCEPT_TAMPER_LOG="$tlog" LAND_GH="$WORK/tamper-gh" bash scripts/land/train.sh watch "$NAME" --interval 1 --timeout 1 2>&1)"; rc=$?
      else
        out="$(cd "$C" && ACCEPT_TAMPER=$mode ACCEPT_REQ="$req" ACCEPT_TAMPER_LOG="$tlog" LAND_GH="$WORK/tamper-gh" bash scripts/land/train.sh --dry-run land "$NAME" 2>&1)"; rc=$?
      fi
      if [ "$rc" = 0 ]; then
        row "neg:$mode" failed "$cmd ACCEPTED a tampered run ($req)"; FAILED=1
      elif [ ! -s "$tlog" ]; then
        row "neg:$mode" failed "$cmd refused, but the wrapper changed nothing: no evidence of the mutation"; FAILED=1
      elif ! printf '%s\n' "$out" | awk -v w="$want" 'index($0, w) {f=1} END {exit !f}'; then
        row "neg:$mode" failed "$cmd refused for another reason (expected '$want'): $(printf '%s\n' "$out" | tail -n 1 | cut -c1-80)"; FAILED=1
      else
        row "neg:$mode" passed "$cmd refused: $want"
      fi
    done
  done
}

make_git_shim
export PATH="$WORK/bin:$PATH"
echo "acceptance: $REF from $ORIGIN_URL"
leg clone do_clone
NAME="${NAME:-accept-$(git -C "$C" rev-parse --short=10 "$REF")}"
leg train do_train
leg build do_build
leg check do_check
leg ci do_ci
leg watch do_watch
leg dryland do_dryland
do_neg_build
do_neg_hook
do_negative
finish
# THE LEGACY ORACLE (ROADMAP R-0564): the old compiler, frozen at one tag.
# Runners get it as a release artifact (fetch.sh: download, check artifact_sha256, verify the
# install); build.sh rebuilds it from a fresh clone of the tag and must reproduce binary_sha256 and
# artifact_sha256, which is what makes the artifact trustworthy. run.sh re-verifies the install
# before each use. Change this file only to re-pin deliberately (a new tag is a new oracle).
# artifact_url is the published release asset (owner approval P13; published 2026-11-05, release tag
# release_tag pointing at commit). fetch.sh downloads it and trusts it only through artifact_sha256.
# Republished on lambdaclass/concrete on 2026-11-07 (the one repository since 2026-11-06), the same
# asset (same artifact_sha256); the first publication stays on the archived concrete2.
tag=legacy-compiler-final
commit=066c9d9b8836a2b176aa37f6c8313195a4b4ee29
url=https://github.com/lambdaclass/concrete.git
build_command=nix develop <checkout> --command lake build concrete
binary=.lake/build/bin/concrete (bin/concrete in the artifact)
lean_toolchain=leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1
flake_lock_sha256=d0a75387719c81fc35e16c0242b7b099f896235959d3936ec510cd2e9c0d9a3d
nixpkgs_rev=aca4d95fce4914b3892661bcb80b8087293536c6
corpus_tests_tree=758c4c89a1fe6f2f785de2264d8995916ec260d3
corpus_examples_tree=b0c78ba592e354606b490721d35daed2d848ebcf
corpus_std_tree=70303f918d3db1cdbf6748054b4112abb68a79e7
lean_version=Lean (version 4.34.1, x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu, commit 5045d0056413266e57c625dcd7c365b10e377c52, Release)
clang_version=clang version 21.1.8
binary_sha256=fa25a30a585d63faa8e65abbc5bd74b668b22f3c0a33731f8d1316b9470aab74
manifest_sha256=7d70da4d74de97e6c54833297b6811597612ee7821d97ecdea6b6afe83fa36fd
artifact_sha256=74d8f1e3b5214d9c64c25736a24c19458736a2dacc7977ff784506839b9f26a2
platform=x86_64-linux
release_repo=lambdaclass/concrete
release_tag=oracle-legacy-compiler-final
artifact_url=https://github.com/lambdaclass/concrete/releases/download/oracle-legacy-compiler-final/oracle-legacy-compiler-final-x86_64-linux.tar.gz
# Reproducibility, 2026-11-04: fresh builds from the public clone reproduced binary_sha256 five times
# (four on concrete-ci-2, Arch, in different directories; one on fede-256gb, Ubuntu) and
# artifact_sha256 four times (three on concrete-ci-2, one on fede-256gb, with different gzip
# versions). concrete-ci-2 caches the install at ~/oracle/<commit>/ (user dev); nothing depends on it.
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Rebuild the legacy oracle (ROADMAP R-0564) from its tag and check it against scripts/oracle/ORACLE.lock.
#
# The oracle is the old compiler, frozen at the tag the lock names. It is built from a FRESH clone of
# a public remote, never from this checkout or from legacy/, so nothing on main and no particular
# host (concrete-ci-2 caches it, but is not required) can change what it is. Each input is checked
# before the build: the tag resolves to the locked commit, the Lean toolchain and flake.lock are the
# locked ones, and the committed build identity is fresh. The binary's sha256 is then compared with
# the lock. A mismatch is a failure: either the build is not reproducible or something changed.
#
#   build.sh            rebuild, verify every locked value, pack the artifact (scripts/oracle/lib.sh),
#                       verify its sha256 too, and install it into $ORACLE_HOME/<commit>/
#   build.sh --record   rebuild and print the measured values in lock syntax (no verification of
#                       binary_sha256 or artifact_sha256); used once to write the lock
#
# The rebuild is the VERIFICATION of the published artifact (fetch.sh is how runners get it): both
# hashes reproduced from a fresh clone of the tag mean the release asset is what the tag builds.
#
# ORACLE_SOURCE   clone source (default: the lock's url); a path or bundle also works
# ORACLE_HOME     install root (default ~/oracle); the install is <commit>/ (the unpacked artifact)
#                 and <commit>.BUILD_INFO beside it
# ORACLE_KEEP=1   keep a failed build's directory for inspection
# ORACLE_ARTIFACT_OUT=PATH  also copy the packed artifact there (for publish.sh)
set -euo pipefail
HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
# shellcheck source=scripts/oracle/lib.sh
. "$HERE/lib.sh"
# The source clone below is a sandbox: no repository variable of the caller may reach it (ISS-0015).
. "$HERE/../checks/lib/git_sandbox.sh"
git_sandbox || exit 2
LOCK="$ORACLE_LOCK"
RECORD=0
[ "${1:-}" = "--record" ] && { RECORD=1; shift; }
[ $# -eq 0 ] || { echo "usage: build.sh [--record]" >&2; exit 2; }
[ -f "$LOCK" ] || { echo "oracle: $LOCK missing" >&2; exit 2; }
die() { echo "oracle: $*" >&2; exit 1; }

TAG="$(lock tag)"; COMMIT="$(lock commit)"
[ -n "$TAG" ] && [ ${#COMMIT} = 40 ] || die "lock has no tag or a short commit"
HOME_DIR="$(oracle_home)"
WORK="$(mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/oracle-build.XXXXXX")"
cleanup() { if [ "${ORACLE_KEEP:-0}" = 1 ]; then echo "oracle: kept $WORK" >&2; else rm -rf "$WORK"; fi; }
trap cleanup EXIT
SRC="$WORK/src"
NIX=(nix --extra-experimental-features "nix-command flakes")

# 1. Source: a fresh clone; the tag must resolve to the locked commit.
cloned=0
for url in ${ORACLE_SOURCE:-} "$(lock url)"; do
  [ -n "$url" ] || continue
  rm -rf "$SRC"
  if git clone --quiet --no-checkout "$url" "$SRC" 2>/dev/null \
     && git -C "$SRC" fetch --quiet origin "refs/tags/$TAG:refs/tags/$TAG" 2>/dev/null; then
    cloned=1; echo "oracle: cloned $url"; break
  fi
done
[ "$cloned" = 1 ] || die "could not clone any source with tag $TAG"
got="$(git -C "$SRC" rev-parse "refs/tags/$TAG^{commit}")"
[ "$got" = "$COMMIT" ] || die "tag $TAG is $got, the lock says $COMMIT (a moved tag is never followed)"
git -C "$SRC" -c advice.detachedHead=false checkout --quiet --detach "$COMMIT"

# 2. Inputs the lock pins.
check() { # key measured
  local want; want="$(lock "$1")"
  [ "$RECORD" = 1 ] && { echo "$1=$2"; return 0; }
  [ "$want" = "$2" ] || die "$1 is '$2', the lock says '$want'"
}
check lean_toolchain "$(tr -d '\n' < "$SRC/lean-toolchain")"
check flake_lock_sha256 "$(sha256sum "$SRC/flake.lock" | cut -d' ' -f1)"
for t in tests examples std; do check "corpus_${t}_tree" "$(git -C "$SRC" rev-parse "$COMMIT:$t")"; done

# 3. The committed build identity must already describe these sources (the binary embeds it).
( cd "$SRC" && bash scripts/gen/build_identity.sh >/dev/null )
[ -z "$(git -C "$SRC" status --porcelain)" ] || { git -C "$SRC" status --short >&2; die "the tag's committed build identity is stale"; }

# 4. Build inside the tag's own default dev shell (its flake.lock pins clang and Lean).
echo "oracle: building $TAG ($COMMIT) in $SRC"
t0=$(date +%s)
( cd "$SRC" && XDG_CACHE_HOME="$WORK/cache" "${NIX[@]}" develop "$SRC" --command lake build concrete ) > "$WORK/build.log" 2>&1 \
  || { tail -40 "$WORK/build.log" >&2; die "lake build failed"; }
BIN="$SRC/.lake/build/bin/concrete"
[ -x "$BIN" ] || die "no binary at .lake/build/bin/concrete"
toolver() { ( cd "$SRC" && XDG_CACHE_HOME="$WORK/cache" "${NIX[@]}" develop "$SRC" --command bash -c "$1" ) 2>/dev/null | head -1; }
LEANV="$(toolver 'lean --version')"; CLANGV="$(toolver 'clang --version')"
check lean_version "$LEANV"
check clang_version "$CLANGV"
SHA="$(sha256sum "$BIN" | cut -d' ' -f1)"
[ "$RECORD" = 1 ] || check binary_sha256 "$SHA"

# 5. Pack the artifact from the verified checkout: the binary, std/ exactly as committed (git
#    archive, so nothing untracked rides along) and the dev shell's flake files.
STAGE="$WORK/stage"; mkdir -p "$STAGE/bin"
cp "$BIN" "$STAGE/bin/concrete"
git -C "$SRC" archive "$COMMIT" std | tar -x -C "$STAGE"
mkdir -p "$STAGE/shell"
git -C "$SRC" archive "$COMMIT" flake.nix flake.lock lean-toolchain | tar -x -C "$STAGE/shell"
TB="$WORK/$(oracle_artifact_name)"
oracle_pack "$STAGE" "$TB"
ASHA="$(sha256sum "$TB" | cut -d' ' -f1)"
MSHA="$(sha256sum "$STAGE/MANIFEST.sha256" | cut -d' ' -f1)"
if [ "$RECORD" = 1 ]; then
  echo "binary_sha256=$SHA"; echo "manifest_sha256=$MSHA"; echo "artifact_sha256=$ASHA"; echo "# build seconds: $(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))"
  [ -z "${ORACLE_ARTIFACT_OUT:-}" ] || cp "$TB" "$ORACLE_ARTIFACT_OUT"
  exit 0
fi
check manifest_sha256 "$MSHA"
check artifact_sha256 "$ASHA"
[ -z "${ORACLE_ARTIFACT_OUT:-}" ] || cp "$TB" "$ORACLE_ARTIFACT_OUT"

# 6. Install through the same path a fetched artifact takes.
DEST="$HOME_DIR/$COMMIT"; mkdir -p "$HOME_DIR"
why="$(oracle_install "$TB" "$DEST")" || die "install: $why"
printf 'tag=%s\ncommit=%s\nbinary_sha256=%s\nartifact_sha256=%s\nsource=rebuilt from the tag\nbuilt_utc=%s\nhost=%s\nbuild_seconds=%s\n' \
  "$TAG" "$COMMIT" "$SHA" "$ASHA" "$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" "$(hostname)" "$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))" > "$HOME_DIR/$COMMIT.BUILD_INFO"
echo "oracle: verified binary $SHA and artifact $ASHA, installed at $DEST"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Publish main: push the exact SHA of HEAD to the one repository's main and confirm it landed.
#
# ONE REPOSITORY (2026-11-06, the owner's decision: "migrating everything to lambdaclass/concrete").
# Until then this script pushed the gated primary (unbalancedparentheses/concrete2) and then
# fast-forwarded a mirror (lambdaclass/concrete) to exactly the primary's tip. There is no mirror
# now: `origin` is lambdaclass/concrete. The name is kept so the documented command and the shared
# lock stay the same. Landings go through scripts/land/train.sh; this script is the by-hand
# publisher, and shares the train's lock.
#
# Kept from the mirror era, each written after a measured mistake:
#   * push the RECORDED SHA, never `HEAD` (HEAD moved during a CI wait and the wrong commit was
#     published);
#   * read the ref back with ls-remote: a push's exit status is not its result;
#   * the CI wait is OPT-IN (--ci-wait; owner, 2026-08-07: "stop waiting on CI in push-both"). Most
#     waits ended `cancelled` because a later push superseded the run. A cancelled run is NO
#     VERDICT, not a red one. The compensating control is the CI-health ritual: check
#     `gh run list --workflow CI` after pushing and treat red as stop-the-line;
#   * the CI query is by full 40-character COMMIT and `--event push`: a branch query races other
#     pushes, an abbreviated SHA returns [] instead of an error, and a scheduled run on the same SHA
#     masked the push run's verdict;
#   * the wait is bounded (CI_TIMEOUT, calibrated above the observed run time), and absence of a
#     run is reported as UNVERIFIED, never as success.
set -uo pipefail
ROOT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
cd "$ROOT_DIR"

# EXCLUSIVE, repo-wide. Two publishes at once is not hypothetical: on 2026-07-31
# two agents sharing a worktree both ran this script and both sat in the CI-wait
# loop for the same SHA. That instance was harmless (same commit, fast-forward,
# idempotent), but the general case is not — two runs can push different tips, and the
# train's land holds the same lock while it checks main and pushes.
#
# The lock lives in the COMMON git dir, not the worktree, because remotes are
# shared across worktrees: two different worktrees publishing at once is exactly
# the case a per-worktree lock would miss.
COMMON_GIT_DIR="$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir 2>/dev/null || echo .git)"
PUSH_LOCK="$COMMON_GIT_DIR/concrete-push-both.lock"
if ! mkdir "$PUSH_LOCK" 2>/dev/null; then
  echo "error: another push-both holds $PUSH_LOCK" >&2
  echo "       remotes are shared across worktrees, so two publishes can race." >&2
  echo "       Wait for it, or if no run is active:" >&2
  echo "         rmdir $PUSH_LOCK" >&2
  if [ -f "$PUSH_LOCK/owner" ]; then
    echo "       holder: $(cat "$PUSH_LOCK/owner" 2>/dev/null)" >&2
  fi
  exit 2
fi
printf 'pid=%s worktree=%s\n' "$$" "$(pwd)" > "$PUSH_LOCK/owner" 2>/dev/null || true
trap 'rm -f "$PUSH_LOCK/owner" 2>/dev/null; rmdir "$PUSH_LOCK" 2>/dev/null || true' EXIT

REMOTE="${PUSH_REMOTE:-origin}"
if [ "$REMOTE" = "fork" ]; then
  echo "push-both: refusing the remote named fork (a divergent repository)" >&2
  exit 2
fi
REPO="${LAND_REPO:-lambdaclass/concrete}"
BRANCH="${BRANCH:-main}"
LOCAL="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"
CI_WAIT=0
CI_WORKFLOW="${CI_WORKFLOW:-CI}"
# Bounded so this cannot hang a session forever; a timeout is reported as no verdict.
#
# CALIBRATED against observed runs, not guessed. A first version used 2400s
# (40min) and timed out on a run that was still in progress — this workflow's
# recent full runs took 41m42s and 42m39s, so the timeout was SHORTER than the
# job it was waiting for. A timeout below the expected duration does not bound a
# hang, it manufactures a failure on every healthy push. 4200s leaves real
# headroom above ~43min while still being finite.
CI_TIMEOUT="${CI_TIMEOUT:-4200}"
CI_INTERVAL="${CI_INTERVAL:-30}"

while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do
  case "$1" in
    --no-ci-wait) CI_WAIT=0; shift ;;          # retained: scripts may still pass it
    --ci-wait)    CI_WAIT=1; shift ;;          # opt in to blocking on the verdict
    *) echo "push-both: unknown option $1" >&2; exit 2 ;;
  esac
done

echo "push-both: ${LOCAL:0:8} to $REMOTE/$BRANCH"
# Push the RECORDED SHA, not `HEAD`. `HEAD` is a moving ref: everything below —
# the read-back of the remote branch and the optional CI wait — is about $LOCAL,
# so pushing HEAD means publishing something other than what was checked.
if ! git push "$REMOTE" "$LOCAL:$BRANCH"; then
  echo "push-both: the push to $REMOTE failed." >&2
  exit 1
fi

# A successful exit is not the same as the ref being where we think.
got="$(git ls-remote "$REMOTE" "refs/heads/$BRANCH" | cut -f1)"
if [ "$got" != "$LOCAL" ]; then
  echo "push-both: $REMOTE/$BRANCH is $got, expected $LOCAL." >&2
  exit 1
fi

# ------------------------------------------------------------------
# Wait for CI on exactly this commit (opt-in).
# ------------------------------------------------------------------
if [ "$CI_WAIT" -eq 1 ]; then
  if ! command -v gh >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "push-both: gh not available, cannot confirm CI for ${LOCAL:0:8}." >&2
    exit 1
  fi
  echo "push-both: waiting for $CI_WORKFLOW on ${LOCAL:0:8} (timeout ${CI_TIMEOUT}s)"
  deadline=$(( $(date +%s) + CI_TIMEOUT ))
  conclusion=""
  while :; do
    # Query by COMMIT, not by branch: a branch query races with anyone else's
    # push and could report a green run for a different tip.
    # FILTER TO THE PUSH EVENT. `--commit` alone matches ANY run for that SHA,
    # including `schedule`-triggered ones — and a nightly run sitting `pending`
    # on the same tip made this wait time out at 4200s while the push run had
    # already finished SUCCESS. Measured on efafe5e2: two schedule runs
    # (pending, in_progress) ahead of a completed push run in the same list.
    row="$(gh run list -R "$REPO" --workflow "$CI_WORKFLOW" --commit "$LOCAL" --event push \
             --limit 1 --json status,conclusion,url 2>/dev/null || true)"
    status="$(sed -n 's/.*"status":"\([^"]*\)".*/\1/p' <<<"$row")"
    conclusion="$(sed -n 's/.*"conclusion":"\([^"]*\)".*/\1/p' <<<"$row")"
    if [ "$status" = "completed" ]; then break; fi
    # An empty result is NOT a status. `gh run list --commit` requires the full
    # 40-char SHA: given an abbreviation it returns [] instead of erroring, so an
    # empty list is indistinguishable from "the run has not been created yet" and
    # from "the workflow name is wrong". Distinguish them explicitly rather than
    # letting all three fall through to a 'pending'-looking timeout.
    if [ -z "$status" ]; then
      if [ "${#LOCAL}" -ne 40 ]; then
        echo "push-both: internal error — CI query needs a full 40-char SHA, got '${LOCAL}'." >&2
        exit 1
      fi
      saw_no_run=1
    else
      saw_no_run=0
    fi
    if [ "$(date +%s)" -ge "$deadline" ]; then
      if [ "${saw_no_run:-0}" -eq 1 ]; then
        echo "push-both: no CI run found for ${LOCAL:0:8} on workflow '$CI_WORKFLOW' (event=push) within ${CI_TIMEOUT}s." >&2
        echo "push-both: treating absence as UNVERIFIED, not as success." >&2
        exit 1
      fi
      echo "push-both: CI for ${LOCAL:0:8} did not conclude within ${CI_TIMEOUT}s (status='${status:-none}')." >&2
      exit 1
    fi
    sleep "$CI_INTERVAL"
  done
  # A CANCELLED run is NO VERDICT, not a red one, and saying "red" sends the reader to
  # investigate a failure that did not happen. Both cancellations seen on 2026-08-06 were
  # environmental: one run superseded by a later push (the workflow sets
  # `cancel-in-progress` for pushes on a ref), one killed by a GitHub outage resolving
  # action downloads. Every job showed `cancelled`; nothing was compiled.
  #
  # Still exits non-zero — no verdict is not a pass — but it says
  # what happened and what to do, which is RE-RUN rather than debug.
  case "$conclusion" in
    success) : ;;
    cancelled|skipped|"")
      echo "push-both: CI for ${LOCAL:0:8} was '${conclusion:-absent}' — NO VERDICT, not a failure." >&2
      echo "push-both: a run is cancelled when a later push supersedes it, or when the" >&2
      echo "           runner cannot start (action-download outages look like this). Nothing" >&2
      echo "           was compiled, so this says nothing about the commit." >&2
      echo "push-both: re-run it — 'gh run rerun -R $REPO \$(gh run list -R $REPO --commit ${LOCAL} --limit 1 --json databaseId -q .[0].databaseId)'" >&2
      exit 1 ;;
    *)
      echo "push-both: CI for ${LOCAL:0:8} concluded '$conclusion'." >&2
      echo "push-both: main is red. Stop the line and fix it." >&2
      exit 1 ;;
  esac
  echo "push-both: CI green on ${LOCAL:0:8}"
else
  # Not waiting is the default now. Say so, and hand over the exact commands to check the
  # verdict later — an unwatched run is only acceptable if checking it is trivial.
  echo "push-both: not waiting for CI (default since 2026-08-07; --ci-wait to block)."
  echo "push-both: check it when convenient —"
  echo "    gh run list -R $REPO --workflow $CI_WORKFLOW --commit $LOCAL --event push --limit 1 \\"
  echo "      --json status,conclusion,url -q '.[0]|\"\\(.status) \\(.conclusion) \\(.url)\"'"
  echo "push-both: a 'cancelled' verdict is NO VERDICT — a later push superseded the run."
fi

echo "push-both: published ${LOCAL:0:8} on $REMOTE/$BRANCH"

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# THE LANDING SCRIPT RESOLVES ONLY WHAT IS MECHANICAL AND REFUSES EVERYTHING ELSE (R-0539).
#
# scripts/land/train.sh builds one integrated candidate from several branches and lands it. The
# hand-run version of that job, on 2026-10-22, made the mistakes this gate pins down: a rebase piped
# to `tail` kept going and a commit was made mid-rebase; union-merged ledgers kept stale entries
# (two OPEN corpus entries for fixed bugs); and candidates were pushed without checking that the CI
# result described them. Each leg below drives the real script against a THROWAWAY repository with
# bare-repo remotes and a fake `gh`, so nothing here can touch this repository, its remotes or CI.
#
#   resolution  generated views (a ROADMAP generated block, a whole generated file) are regenerated,
#               ledgers are union-merged; the train carries both members' entries
#   refusal     a conflict in source or in prose outside a generated block aborts the merge (no
#               MERGE_HEAD left, exit 3); a union that duplicates a corpus key is refused; a
#               merge or rebase already in progress, a dirty tree and an existing train are refused
#   pipeline    check feeds the hook exactly base..head on ci/<name>; ci pushes and records the run
#   watch       a campaign-only failure is reported as such; a landing failure fetches log tails
#   land        refused on a head-SHA mismatch, a red landing job, a moved main, a remote named fork;
#               dry-run pushes nothing; a real land with no lambdaclass remote pushes origin exactly
#               once and no second remote, and cancels no run (main's own run records the train's, R-0570)
#   record      (2026-11-06, R-0570) main's run names the run that qualified its exact SHA only when
#               the newest completed dispatch of the CI workflow at that SHA AND at that base (its
#               title, from the base input train.sh ci passes) passed every landing-tier and required
#               job and the head is ahead of the base; a missing run, a run at another SHA or base, of
#               another workflow or event, unfinished or excluded, a skipped,
#               cancelled or failed job, an older green run behind a newer red one, a diverged or
#               absent base: no record. The workflow gates every job on it (structure check)
#   dropped     (2026-10-25) a push that fails before the remote moved is retried; one that fails
#               after it moved is accepted from ls-remote; exhausted retries fail and move nothing
#   bisect      halving finds a seeded red member
#   report      the median time to land is computed from the log
#   gate host   (2026-11-05) check's impact leg on the gate host: bound to the train head's SHA (a
#               run at another SHA, an unreachable host or a lost log is INCOMPLETE and blocks; only
#               --local-gates runs here); the hook reuses the attestation only for this exact push, and
#               a tampered log or attestation is refused
#   review      (2026-11-06) review fails closed: check with no review command fails; a reviewer that
#               exits 0 but leaves no note, a malformed, blocked or findings note fails the leg; check
#               skips the reviewer when every member is reviewed clean; land requires a clean head
#               note, or (head unreviewed) a clean note on every member, and refuses an absent,
#               malformed, blocked or findings note in either place
#   required    (2026-11-04) a required job (required-checks.txt) that is missing, skipped or
#               cancelled refuses watch and land; a candidate drops a requirement main imposes only
#               through a transition row (replaced by jobs it requires and that pass, or retired with
#               a reason and reported as retired); the file names the workflow's landing-tier jobs
set -uo pipefail
source "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/lib/gate_env.sh" || exit 2
ROOT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/../.." && pwd)"
TRAIN="$ROOT_DIR/scripts/land/train.sh"
REPORT="$ROOT_DIR/scripts/land/report.sh"
[ -f "$TRAIN" ] && [ -f "$REPORT" ] || { echo "FATAL: scripts/land/train.sh or report.sh missing" >&2; exit 2; }
TMP="$(mktemp -d)" || { echo "FATAL: could not create a sandbox directory" >&2; exit 2; }
[ -n "$TMP" ] && [ -d "$TMP" ] || { echo "FATAL: sandbox directory is not usable" >&2; exit 2; }
trap 'rm -rf "$TMP"' EXIT
PASS=0; FAIL=0
ok(){ echo "  ok   $1"; PASS=$((PASS+1)); }
no(){ echo "  FAIL $1"; FAIL=$((FAIL+1)); }

# Isolation: no inherited repository (the hook leaks GIT_DIR), no user or system git config (hooks
# path, conflict style, signing), no toolchain re-entry.
source "$ROOT_DIR/scripts/checks/lib/git_sandbox.sh" || exit 2
git_sandbox "$TMP/gitconfig" || exit 2
cat > "$GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL" <<EOF
[user]
	name = landing gate
	email = gate@example.invalid
[init]
	defaultBranch = main
[core]
	hooksPath = $TMP/nohooks
[merge]
	conflictStyle = diff3
EOF
mkdir -p "$TMP/nohooks"
# train.sh runs only with the repository's pre-push hook enabled (ISS-0015): it asks the hook git
# would run who it is (its single argument --concrete-identify). The sandbox's global hooksPath holds this
# stand-in, which runs no gate and answers as the repository's .githooks/pre-push would: a test
# fixture impersonating the hook on purpose.
IDENTIFY='[ "$#" = 1 ] && [ "$1" = --concrete-identify ] && { echo "concrete-pre-push $(cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)" && pwd -P)/.githooks/pre-push"; exit 0; }'
standin_hook() {
  printf '#!/usr/bin/env bash\n%s\nexit 0\n' "$IDENTIFY" > "$TMP/nohooks/pre-push"
  chmod +x "$TMP/nohooks/pre-push"
}
standin_hook
export LAND_NO_NIX=1 LAND_STATE_DIR="$TMP/state" LAND_GH="$TMP/fake-gh" LAND_REPO=test/repo
export LAND_PUSH_RETRY_SLEEP=0
unset LAND_GATES_HOST LAND_GATES_TRANSPORT CONCRETE_PREPUSH_NO_REUSE
export FAKE_GH_DIR="$TMP/gh"; mkdir -p "$FAKE_GH_DIR"

cat > "$TMP/fake-gh" <<'GH'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# A fake gh: run state comes from $FAKE_GH_DIR, every call is appended to calls.log.
echo "$*" >> "$FAKE_GH_DIR/calls.log"
case "$1 $2" in
  "workflow run") exit 0 ;;
  "run list") cat "$FAKE_GH_DIR/list.json" ;;
  "run cancel") exit 0 ;;
  "run view") if [ -f "$FAKE_GH_DIR/run.$3.json" ]; then cat "$FAKE_GH_DIR/run.$3.json"; else cat "$FAKE_GH_DIR/run.json"; fi ;;
  "api repos/test/repo/compare/"*) cat "$FAKE_GH_DIR/compare.json" 2>/dev/null || exit 1 ;;
  "api repos/test/repo/actions/runs?"*) cat "$FAKE_GH_DIR/runs.json" 2>/dev/null || exit 1 ;;
  "api "*) echo "fake log tail for $2" ;;
  *) echo "fake gh: unexpected $*" >&2; exit 1 ;;
esac
GH
chmod +x "$TMP/fake-gh"

# A review note as scripts/review/review.sh writes it (scripts/review/verify.sh's six keys), published
# to origin. put-review <sha> <clean|findings|blocked|malformed|none>[@rounds]; none removes the note.
cat > "$TMP/put-review" <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -e
sha="$(git rev-parse "$1")"; R=refs/notes/concrete-review
git fetch -q origin "+$R:$R" 2>/dev/null || true
case "$2" in
  none) git notes --ref=$R remove --ignore-missing "$sha" 2>/dev/null ;;
  malformed) git notes --ref=$R add -f -m "verdict: clean" "$sha" ;;
  *) git notes --ref=$R add -f -m "verdict: ${2%@*}
rounds: $(case "$2" in *@*) echo "${2#*@}" ;; *) echo 1 ;; esac)
reviewer: codex
utc: 2026-11-06T00:00:00Z
base: $sha
pieces: 1" "$sha" ;;
esac
git show-ref -q --verify $R || exit 0
CONCRETE_SKIP_GATES=1 git push -q origin $R
EOF
# The review command of every check (LAND_REVIEW_CMD): called with --base B --head H, it logs the
# call and answers per $FAKE_GH_DIR/review.mode: a note of that kind on H (default clean), none (exit
# 0, writes nothing) or fail (exit 1, writes nothing).
cat > "$TMP/fake-review" <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
mode="$(cat "$FAKE_GH_DIR/review.mode" 2>/dev/null || echo clean)"
echo "$4 $mode" >> "$FAKE_GH_DIR/review.log"
case "$mode" in none) exit 0 ;; fail) exit 1 ;; esac
bash "$(dirname "$0")/put-review" "$4" "$mode"
EOF
export LAND_REVIEW_CMD="bash $TMP/fake-review"

W="$TMP/work"
for r in origin fork; do git init -q --bare "$TMP/$r.git"; done
git init -q "$W"
cd "$W" || exit 2
for r in origin fork; do git remote add "$r" "$TMP/$r.git"; done

# --- the toy repository: a ledger, a corpus map, a generated block, a generated file ---------------
mkdir -p scripts/gen scripts/tests docs/project src Concrete
BEGIN='<!-- BEGIN GENERATED: candidate record (scripts/gen/candidate_table.py; edit docs/project/candidates.tsv) -->'
END='<!-- END GENERATED: candidate record -->'
printf '# Changelog\n\n- base entry\n' > CHANGELOG.md
cat > scripts/tests/audit_bug_corpus.sh <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
declare -A BUG_TEST_MAP=(
  [100]="regress_100.con"
  [110]="regress_110.con"
)
declare -A SKIP_BUGS=(
  [050]="OPEN -- base"
)
EOF
printf 'id\tnote\nc0\tbase\n' > docs/project/candidates.tsv
# A second generated block whose generator does not exist: nothing may resolve a conflict in it.
GHOST='<!-- BEGIN GENERATED: ghost view (scripts/gen/ghost.py; edit nothing) -->'
printf '# Roadmap\n\nprose line one\n\n%s\n%s\n\nprose line two\n\n%s\nghost\n%s\n' "$BEGIN" "$END" "$GHOST" '<!-- END GENERATED: ghost view -->' > ROADMAP.md
cat > scripts/gen/candidate_table.py <<'EOF'
import re
import sys
rows = [l.split("\t") for l in open("docs/project/candidates.tsv").read().splitlines()[1:] if l]
if any(r[0] == "BROKEN" for r in rows):
    sys.exit("candidate record: BROKEN row refused")
body = "\n".join(f"| {r[0]} | {r[1]} |" for r in sorted(rows))
s = open("ROADMAP.md").read()
b = s.index("<!-- BEGIN GENERATED: candidate record"); b = s.index("\n", b) + 1
e = s.index("<!-- END GENERATED: candidate record -->")
open("ROADMAP.md", "w").write(s[:b] + body + "\n" + s[e:])
EOF
cat > scripts/gen/build_identity.sh <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail
printf 'def digest := "%s"\n' "$(cat src/*.txt | git hash-object --stdin)" > Concrete/BuildIdentity.lean
EOF
printf 'one\n' > src/one.txt; printf 'two\n' > src/two.txt
printf '# required\nBuild\n' > required-checks.txt
regen(){ python3 -I scripts/gen/candidate_table.py && bash scripts/gen/build_identity.sh; }
regen
git add -A && git commit -qm base
git push -q origin main && git push -q fork main
BASE="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"

branch(){ git checkout -q -b "$1" "$BASE"; }
commit_all(){ regen; git add -A; git commit -qm "$1"; }
corpus_add(){ python3 -I - "$1" <<'EOF'
import sys
p = "scripts/tests/audit_bug_corpus.sh"; s = open(p).read()
s = s.replace('  [100]="regress_100.con"\n', '  [100]="regress_100.con"\n' + sys.argv[1] + "\n", 1)
open(p, "w").write(s)
EOF
}
# b1, b2: ledger, corpus, generated-block and generated-file conflicts with each other only.
changelog_add(){ python3 -I -c 'import sys; p="CHANGELOG.md"; s=open(p).read(); open(p,"w").write(s.replace("- base entry", sys.argv[1] + "\n- base entry", 1))' "$1"; }
branch b1; changelog_add '- b1 entry'
corpus_add '  [201]="regress_201.con"'; printf 'c1\tfrom b1\n' >> docs/project/candidates.tsv
printf 'one b1\n' > src/one.txt; commit_all b1
branch b2; changelog_add '- b2 entry'
corpus_add '  [202]="regress_202.con"'; printf 'c2\tfrom b2\n' >> docs/project/candidates.tsv
printf 'two b2\n' > src/two.txt; commit_all b2
# b3: a source conflict with b1. b4/b5: a prose conflict outside the generated block.
branch b3; printf 'one b3\n' > src/one.txt; commit_all b3
branch b4; sed 's/prose line two/prose line two (b4)/' ROADMAP.md > r && mv r ROADMAP.md; commit_all b4
branch b5; sed 's/prose line two/prose line two (b5)/' ROADMAP.md > r && mv r ROADMAP.md; commit_all b5
corpus_after(){ python3 -I - "$1" "$2" <<'EOF'
import sys
p = "scripts/tests/audit_bug_corpus.sh"; s = open(p).read()
anchor = sys.argv[1] + "\n"
s = s.replace(anchor, anchor + sys.argv[2] + "\n", 1)
open(p, "w").write(s)
EOF
}
corpus_edit(){ sed "s/$1/$2/" scripts/tests/audit_bug_corpus.sh > a && mv a scripts/tests/audit_bug_corpus.sh; }
# b6/b7: both add [150], at different places: a clean merge that duplicates a key (bash keeps one).
branch b6; corpus_after '  [100]="regress_100.con"' '  [150]="regress_150a.con"'; commit_all b6
branch b7; corpus_after '  [110]="regress_110.con"' '  [150]="regress_150b.con"'; commit_all b7
# b10/b11: both re-key [100]: an edit conflict, never a union.
branch b10; corpus_edit 'regress_100.con' 'regress_100b.con'; commit_all b10
branch b11; corpus_edit 'regress_100.con' 'regress_100c.con'; commit_all b11
# b12/b13: both insert code after the maps: never unioned in a map-shaped ledger.
branch b12; echo 'echo twelve' >> scripts/tests/audit_bug_corpus.sh; commit_all b12
branch b13; echo 'echo thirteen' >> scripts/tests/audit_bug_corpus.sh; commit_all b13
# b17/b18: both insert inside a map, one line smuggling code after a well-formed key.
branch b17; corpus_after '  [100]="regress_100.con"' '  [170]="x" ); echo smuggled; BUG_TEST_MAP+=('; commit_all b17
branch b18; corpus_after '  [100]="regress_100.con"' '  [180]="regress_180.con"'; commit_all b18
# b14/b15: both edit the ghost block, whose generator does not exist.
branch b14; sed 's/^ghost$/ghost 14/' ROADMAP.md > r && mv r ROADMAP.md; commit_all b14
branch b15; sed 's/^ghost$/ghost 15/' ROADMAP.md > r && mv r ROADMAP.md; commit_all b15
# b16: a record the generator refuses (committed without regenerating, as a broken branch would be).
branch b16; printf 'BROKEN\tx\n' >> docs/project/candidates.tsv; git add -A; git commit -qm b16
# b8: the seeded red member for bisection (adds a file the "test" forbids).
branch b8; printf 'bad\n' > BAD; commit_all b8
branch b9; printf 'nine\n' > nine.txt; commit_all b9
branch b19; printf 'nineteen\n' > nineteen.txt; commit_all b19
git checkout -q main

no_op_left(){ for p in MERGE_HEAD CHERRY_PICK_HEAD REVERT_HEAD rebase-merge rebase-apply; do [ -e "$(git rev-parse --git-path "$p")" ] && return 1; done; return 0; }
show(){ git show "train/$1:$2" 2>/dev/null; }
has(){ case "$1" in *"$2"*) return 0 ;; esac; return 1; }

echo "=== resolution: generated views regenerated, ledgers union-merged ==="
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" build t1 --no-fetch b1 b2 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" = 0 ] && ok "build t1 (b1 b2) succeeds despite four conflicting files" || { no "build t1 exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
no_op_left && ok "no merge left in progress" || no "a merge is still in progress after build t1"
# Not vacuous: each of the four files really conflicted and took the intended route.
has "$out" "resolved CHANGELOG.md: union" && has "$out" "resolved scripts/tests/audit_bug_corpus.sh: union" \
  && has "$out" "resolved ROADMAP.md: every hunk is inside a generated block" \
  && has "$out" "resolved Concrete/BuildIdentity.lean: generated whole" \
  && ok "the four conflicts were real and each took its route" || { no "resolution routes"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
cl="$(show t1 CHANGELOG.md)"
has "$cl" "- b1 entry" && has "$cl" "- b2 entry" && ok "CHANGELOG carries both members' entries" || no "CHANGELOG lost an entry"
corp="$(show t1 scripts/tests/audit_bug_corpus.sh)"
has "$corp" "[201]=" && has "$corp" "[202]=" && ok "corpus map carries [201] and [202]" || no "corpus map lost a key"
rm_="$(show t1 ROADMAP.md)"
has "$rm_" "| c1 | from b1 |" && has "$rm_" "| c2 | from b2 |" && ok "generated block regenerated from both members' records" || no "generated block is not the union view"
want="$(printf 'def digest := "%s"' "$(printf 'one b1\ntwo b2\n' | git hash-object --stdin)")"
[ "$(show t1 Concrete/BuildIdentity.lean)" = "$want" ] && ok "BuildIdentity regenerated over the merged sources" || no "BuildIdentity is stale on the train"
if git grep -qE '^(<{7}|>{7}|={7})( |$)' train/t1 -- . ; then no "conflict markers committed on the train"; else ok "no conflict markers committed"; fi
has "$(git log --format=%s "$BASE..train/t1")" "train/t1: merge b2" && ok "merge commits name member and SHA" || no "merge commit message"
st="$(cat "$TMP/state/t1/train.env")"
has "$st" "head=$(git rev-parse train/t1)" && has "$st" "b1@$(git rev-parse b1)" && ok "state binds head and member SHAs" || no "state file: $st"
log="$(cat "$TMP/state/log/t1.tsv" 2>/dev/null)"
has "$log" "	build	" && has "$log" "	passed	" && ok "build row logged" || no "no build row in the log"

echo "=== refusal: real conflicts stop the merge and leave nothing in progress ==="
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" build t2 --no-fetch b1 b3 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" = 3 ] && has "$out" "REAL CONFLICT src/one.txt" && ok "a source conflict stops with exit 3 and names the file" || { no "source conflict: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
no_op_left && ok "the stopped merge was aborted (no MERGE_HEAD)" || no "MERGE_HEAD left behind"
[ -z "$(git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no)" ] && ok "tree clean after the stop" || no "tree dirty after the stop"
[ "$(git rev-parse train/t2^2)" = "$(git rev-parse b1)" ] && ok "train/t2 holds the members merged before the conflict" || no "train/t2 head unexpected"
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" build t3 --no-fetch b4 b5 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" = 3 ] && has "$out" "outside every generated block" && ok "a prose conflict outside the generated block is refused" || { no "prose conflict: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
no_op_left || no "MERGE_HEAD left behind after the prose conflict"
git checkout -q main
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" build t4 --no-fetch b6 b7 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" != 0 ] && has "$out" "duplicate key [150]" && ok "a merge that duplicates a corpus key is refused" || { no "duplicate corpus key accepted (exit $rc)"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
no_op_left || no "operation left in progress after the duplicate-key refusal"
git checkout -q main
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" build t4 --force --no-fetch b10 b11 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" = 3 ] && has "$out" "edit the same existing lines" && ok "two edits of one ledger entry are a real conflict, not a union" || { no "ledger edit conflict: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" build t4 --force --no-fetch b12 b13 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" = 3 ] && has "$out" "outside every declare -A map" && ok "code inserted outside the corpus maps is never unioned" || { no "corpus code union: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" build t4 --force --no-fetch b17 b18 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" = 3 ] && has "$out" "not a map entry" && ok "a map line carrying code after its key is never unioned" || { no "smuggled code: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" build t4 --force --no-fetch b14 b15 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" = 3 ] && has "$out" "which the train will not run" && ok "a generated block whose generator will not run is a real conflict" || { no "ghost block: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" build t4 --force --no-fetch b16 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" != 0 ] && has "$out" "a generator refused" && [ -z "$(git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no)" ] \
  && ok "a generator's refusal fails the build and leaves the tree clean" || { no "generator refusal: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
has "$(cat "$TMP/state/t4/train.env")" "head=" && no "a refused build recorded a head" || ok "a refused build records no head"
no_op_left || no "operation left in progress after the generator refusal"
git checkout -q main
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" build t1 --no-fetch b1 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" != 0 ] && has "$out" "exists" && ok "an existing train is not overwritten without --force" || no "existing train overwritten (exit $rc)"
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" build 'bad/name' --no-fetch b1 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" != 0 ] && ok "a train name with '/' is refused" || no "bad name accepted"
echo dirty >> CHANGELOG.md
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" build t5 --no-fetch b1 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" != 0 ] && has "$out" "uncommitted" && ok "a dirty tree is refused" || no "dirty tree accepted"
git checkout -q -- CHANGELOG.md
git checkout -q -b scratch b1; git merge -q b3 >/dev/null 2>&1
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" build t6 --no-fetch b2 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" != 0 ] && has "$out" "in progress (MERGE_HEAD" && ok "a merge already in progress is refused" || { no "in-progress merge not detected (exit $rc)"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
git merge --abort; git checkout -q main
git checkout -q --detach b2; git rebase -q b3 >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" build t6 --no-fetch b2 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" != 0 ] && has "$out" "in progress" && ok "a rebase in progress is refused" || { no "rebase in progress not detected (exit $rc)"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
git rebase --abort 2>/dev/null; git checkout -q -f main

echo "=== pipeline: check -> ci -> watch -> land, with a fake gh ==="
git checkout -q train/t1
HEAD1="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"
cat > "$TMP/hook" <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
cat > "$FAKE_GH_DIR/hook.stdin"
[ ! -f "$FAKE_GH_DIR/hook.fail" ]
EOF
export LAND_HOOK="$TMP/hook"
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" ci t1 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" != 0 ] && has "$out" "no passing check" && ok "ci refuses a train with no passing check" || no "ci ran without a check"
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" check t1 --no-remote 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" = 0 ] && [ "$(cat "$FAKE_GH_DIR/hook.stdin")" = "refs/heads/train/t1 $HEAD1 refs/heads/ci/t1 $BASE" ] \
  && ok "check feeds the hook exactly base..head on ci/t1" || { no "check: exit $rc, hook stdin '$(cat "$FAKE_GH_DIR/hook.stdin" 2>/dev/null)'"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
touch "$FAKE_GH_DIR/hook.fail"
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" check t1 --no-remote 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" != 0 ] && ok "check fails when the hook's selection fails" || no "check passed over a failing hook"
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" ci t1 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" != 0 ] && has "$out" "no passing check" && ok "a failed re-check withdraws the earlier pass" || no "ci used a stale pass after a failed re-check"
rm -f "$FAKE_GH_DIR/hook.fail"
bash "$TRAIN" check t1 --no-remote >/dev/null 2>&1 || no "re-check after repair"

echo "=== gate host: check's impact leg on the gate host, bound to the SHA; INCOMPLETE blocks ==="
# A fake transport in place of gates_transport_ssh.sh. It reads the SHA from the bundle it was given
# (as run-at-ref.sh would), records what it was asked, and answers per $FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.mode.
cat > "$TMP/fake-transport" <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
host="$1" bundle="$2" ref="$3" cmd="$4"
printf '%s\n' "$host" > "$FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.host"
printf '%s\n' "$cmd" > "$FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.cmd"
sha="$(git bundle list-heads "$bundle" "$ref" | cut -d' ' -f1)"
printf '%s\n' "$sha" > "$FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.sha"
mode="$(cat "$FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.mode" 2>/dev/null || echo ok)"
case "$mode" in
  unreachable) echo "ssh: connect to host $host port 22: Connection refused" >&2; exit 255 ;;
  wrongsha) sha="$(cat "$FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.other")" ;;
esac

exec

gh_check(){ # mode -> runs check t1 against the gate host fakehost; output in $out, status in $rc
  printf '%s\n' "$1" > "$FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.mode"
  rm -f "$FAKE_GH_DIR/hook.stdin" "$FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.host" "$FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.cmd"
  out="$(LAND_GATES_HOST=fakehost CONCRETE_TEST_FWD='a b' CONCRETE_SKIP_GATES=1 bash "$TRAIN" check t1 --no-remote 2>&1)"; rc=$?
}
lastrow(){ tail -n 1 "$TMP/state/log/t1.tsv"; }
rowis(){ lastrow | awk -F'\t' -v s="$1" -v v="$2" '$3 == s && $8 == v { f = 1 } END { exit !f }'; } # stage result
gh_check ok
[ "$rc" = 0 ] && [ "$(cat "$FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.host" 2>/dev/null)" = fakehost ] && [ "$(cat "$FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.sha")" = "$HEAD1" ] \
  && ok "check runs the impact leg on the gate host, from a bundle holding exactly the train head" || { no "gate host ok: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
[ ! -f "$FAKE_GH_DIR/hook.stdin" ] && ok "with a gate host the gates do not run on this machine" || no "the local hook ran although the gate host passed"
cmd_="$(cat "$FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.cmd" 2>/dev/null)"
has "$cmd_" "impact_gates.sh --base $BASE --head $HEAD1 --src-ref refs/heads/train/t1 --dst-ref refs/heads/ci/t1" \
  && ok "the host runs the hook's selection over exactly base..head on ci/t1" || no "remote command: $cmd_"
has "$cmd_" 'export CONCRETE_TEST_FWD=a\ b;' && ! has "$cmd_" 'export CONCRETE_SKIP_GATES' \
  && [ "${cmd_%%;*}" = "unset CONCRETE_SKIP_GATES CONCRETE_PREPUSH_REENTERED" ] \
  && ok "CONCRETE_* is forwarded quoted; the bypass is never forwarded and is cleared first" || no "forwarded env: $cmd_"
grep -qx "head=$HEAD1" "$ATT" 2>/dev/null && [ "$(tail -n 1 "$ATT")" = complete=1 ] && [ -f "$ATT.log" ] \
  && ok "the pass is attested for the train head, with the host's log kept" || no "no attestation for $HEAD1 at $ATT"
rowis check passed && has "$(lastrow)" "impact on fakehost" && has "$(cat "$TMP/state/t1/train.env")" "checked=$HEAD1" \
  && ok "the check row names the gate host; the train is checked at its head" || no "check row: $(lastrow)"
printf '%s\n' "$(git rev-parse b1)" > "$FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.other"
gh_check wrongsha
[ "$rc" != 0 ] && has "$out" "INCOMPLETE" && has "$out" "another commit" && [ ! -f "$ATT" ] \
  && ok "a run at another SHA is no result: INCOMPLETE, and the old attestation is withdrawn" || { no "wrong SHA: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
rowis check incomplete && ! has "$(cat "$TMP/state/t1/train.env")" "checked=$HEAD1" \
  && ok "an incomplete check is logged as incomplete and withdraws the earlier pass" || no "incomplete row: $(lastrow)"
out2="$(bash "$TRAIN" ci t1 2>&1)"
has "$out2" "no passing check" && ok "ci refuses after an incomplete check" || no "ci ran after an incomplete check"
gh_check unreachable
[ "$rc" != 0 ] && has "$out" "INCOMPLETE" && has "$out" "--local-gates" && rowis check incomplete && [ ! -f "$FAKE_GH_DIR/hook.stdin" ] \
  && ok "an unreachable gate host is INCOMPLETE, blocks, and never falls back to a local run" || { no "unreachable: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
gh_check lost
[ "$rc" != 0 ] && has "$out" "INCOMPLETE" && rowis check incomplete \
  && ok "a log without its exit line (lost connection) is INCOMPLETE" || { no "lost connection: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
gh_check fail
[ "$rc" != 0 ] && has "$out" "FAILED on fakehost" && rowis check failed \
  && ok "gates that fail on the host at the head fail the check (not incomplete)" || { no "remote failure: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
gh_check norecord
[ "$rc" = 0 ] && has "$out" "no attestation" && [ ! -f "$ATT" ] \
  && ok "a pass without a remote record passes, attests nothing, says the push runs the gates" || { no "norecord: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
printf 'unreachable\n' > "$FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.mode"; rm -f "$FAKE_GH_DIR/hook.stdin" "$FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.host"

  && ok "--local-gates is the explicit opt-in to run the gates on this machine" || { no "--local-gates: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
printf 'ok\n' > "$FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.mode"; rm -f "$FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.host"
git config land.gatesHost cfghost
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" check t1 --no-remote 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" = 0 ] && [ "$(cat "$FAKE_GH_DIR/transport.host" 2>/dev/null)" = cfghost ] && ok "git config land.gatesHost names the gate host" || no "land.gatesHost: exit $rc"

has "$out" "impact gates on cfghost" && ok "dry-run check names the gate host" || no "dry-run: $out"

  && ok "an empty LAND_GATES_HOST overrides the config (no gate host)" || no "empty LAND_GATES_HOST: exit $rc"

mklog(){ # sha exit-rc extra-record-lines... -> $TMP/rlog: a run-at-ref log carrying a record of this push
  local sha="$1" erc="$2"; shift 2
  lib ppr_remote_components "$R" "$sha" "$TMP/changed" "$TMP/plan" "$TMP/rcomp" || return 1
  { echo "[concrete-remote] ref=refs/land-remote/x sha=$sha clone=/runs/x host=gatehost"
    echo "gate output"
    echo "=== concrete-remote-record-begin"
    echo "format=concrete-prepush-record-v1"; cat "$TMP/rcomp"; echo "binary=hostbinary"
    for l in "$@"; do echo "$l"; done
    echo "complete=1"
    echo "=== concrete-remote-record-end"
    echo "[concrete-remote] exit=$erc elapsed=9s sha=$sha"; } > "$TMP/rlog"
}
reuse(){ # sha [changed-file] [plan-file] -> the hook's decision for a push of sha; PPR_REASON on stdout
  ( unset CONCRETE_TEST_FWD; cd "$R" && source "$ROOT_DIR/scripts/checks/lib/prepush_record.sh" \
    && ppr_remote_components "$R" "$1" "${2:-$TMP/changed}" "${3:-$TMP/plan}" "$TMP/pcomp" \
    && ppr_remote_check "$R" "$TMP/pcomp" && echo REUSE || echo "refused:$PPR_REASON" )
}

echo "=== gate host end to end: the real ssh transport, shimmed, carries the command intact ==="
# The real gates_remote.sh -> gates_transport_ssh.sh -> run-at-ref.sh chain, with ssh and scp
# replaced by local shims (the "host" is a directory) and a committed stub impact_gates.sh that
# records its environment and writes the hook's record the way the real hook would, unless the
# bypass is set (then it "skips", exit 0, no record: what the real hook does).
RI="$TMP/ri"; HH="$TMP/gatehost"; mkdir -p "$HH"

  && cat > scripts/land/impact_gates.sh <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# stub: --base B --head H --src-ref S --dst-ref D
env -0 > "$CONCRETE_TEST_OUT/remote.env"
printf '%s\n' "$@" > "$CONCRETE_TEST_OUT/remote.args"
[ -z "${CONCRETE_SKIP_GATES:-}" ] || { echo "pre-push: gates SKIPPED"; exit 0; }

{ echo "format=concrete-prepush-record-v1"; cat "$t/comp"; echo status=pass; echo complete=1; } > "$(git rev-parse --git-path concrete-prepush-record)"
rm -rf "$t"
echo "impact: stub gates passed"
EOF
  git add -A && git commit -qm base && git push -q origin HEAD:main \
  && echo 'echo gate2' > scripts/g2.sh && git add -A && git commit -qm head )

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# scp -r <dir> host:<name>  ->  copy into the fake host's home
src="${@: -2:1}" dst="${@: -1}"
cp -r "$src" "$GATEHOST_HOME/${dst#*:}"
EOF
cat > "$TMP/ssh-shim" <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ssh <host> <command>: the command runs in the fake host's home, through a login shell that is not bash's
# own quoting (sh -c), with the gate bypass exported on the host side.
[ -f "$GATEHOST_HOME/unreachable" ] && { echo "ssh: connect to host $1 port 22: No route to host" >&2; exit 255; }

EOF

e2e(){ # -> runs gates_remote.sh in $RI for base..head; output in $out, status in $rc
  out="$(cd "$RI" && GATEHOST_HOME="$HH" LAND_GATES_SSH="$TMP/ssh-shim" LAND_GATES_SCP="$TMP/scp-shim" \
    CONCRETE_TEST_OUT="$TMP" CONCRETE_TEST_LIB="$ROOT_DIR/scripts/checks/lib/prepush_record.sh" CONCRETE_INJ="$INJ" \
    bash "$ROOT_DIR/scripts/land/gates_remote.sh" --host fakehost --base "$RIB" --head "$RIH" 2>&1)"; rc=$?
}

dec="$( cd "$RI" && source "$ROOT_DIR/scripts/checks/lib/prepush_record.sh" && ppr_remote_components "$RI" "$RIH" "$TMP/ri.changed" "$TMP/ri.plan" "$TMP/ri.comp" \
  && ppr_remote_check "$RI" "$TMP/ri.comp" && echo REUSE || echo "refused:$PPR_REASON" )"

# one without its gate clone is INCOMPLETE and named (2026-11-08: the root admin alias looked for

printf '#!/bin/sh\ncase "$1" in -u) echo 0 ;; -un) echo root ;; *) exit 1 ;; esac\n' > "$TMP/hostroot/id"

  && ok "a gate login without its gate clone is INCOMPLETE and names the account and path" || { no "missing gate clone: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }

  && ok "a gate clone that is a linked worktree runs the gates" || { no "worktree gate clone: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }

[ "$rc" = 3 ] && has "$out" "INCOMPLETE" && [ ! -f "$RIATT" ] && ok "the real transport to an unreachable host is INCOMPLETE (exit 3), attests nothing" || { no "e2e unreachable: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
hook="$ROOT_DIR/.githooks/pre-push"
# The root hook has no gate selection: every push that can reach main or ci/* builds the spec and

# attestation to stand in for. It must therefore never consult one, and must run the full set.
! grep -q "ppr_remote_check\|ppr_remote_components" "$hook" && grep -qxF "lake build >\"\$BUILD_LOG\" 2>&1 || { echo \"pre-push: BUILD FAILED — see \$BUILD_LOG\" >&2; exit 1; }" "$hook" \
  && grep -qF "bash scripts/checks/run.sh ||" "$hook" \
  && ok "the root pre-push hook builds the spec and runs the full root set; it consults no remote attestation" || no "hook wiring"
bash "$TRAIN" check t1 --no-remote >/dev/null 2>&1 || no "re-check after the gate-host legs"

  && [ -z "$(git ls-remote origin refs/heads/ci/t1)" ] \
  && ok "dry-run ci defaults LAND_REPO to lambdaclass/concrete and pushes nothing" || { no "dry-run ci default repository: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
printf '[{"databaseId": 4242, "headSha": "%s", "createdAt": "9999-01-01T00:00:00Z"}]' "$HEAD1" > "$FAKE_GH_DIR/list.json"
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" ci t1 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" = 0 ] && [ "$(git ls-remote origin refs/heads/ci/t1 | cut -f1)" = "$HEAD1" ] && has "$(cat "$TMP/state/t1/train.env")" "run_id=4242" \

  && ok "ci pushes ci/t1 at the head, dispatches it with the train's base, records run 4242" || { no "ci: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }

}

echo "=== dropped: a push's result is read from the remote, and a failed push is retried ==="
# The pre-push hook stands in for GitHub dropping the connection: drop.always fails every push
# before the remote moves; drop.before fails one push before it moves; drop.after updates the
# remote itself and then fails, as a push does whose connection drops after the update.
{ echo '#!/usr/bin/env bash'; echo "$IDENTIFY"; } > "$TMP/nohooks/pre-push"
cat >> "$TMP/nohooks/pre-push" <<'EOF'
# Like the real hook, a push that skips the gates (the ci base reset) runs nothing.
[ "${CONCRETE_SKIP_GATES:-0}" = 1 ] && exit 0
[ -f "$FAKE_GH_DIR/drop.always" ] && exit 1
if [ -f "$FAKE_GH_DIR/drop.before" ]; then rm -f "$FAKE_GH_DIR/drop.before"; exit 1; fi
if [ -f "$FAKE_GH_DIR/drop.after" ]; then
  rm -f "$FAKE_GH_DIR/drop.after"
  read -r _lref lsha rref _rsha
  git -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null push -q "$1" "$lsha:$rref" || exit 3
  exit 1
fi
exit 0
EOF
chmod +x "$TMP/nohooks/pre-push"
git fetch -q origin
git checkout -q main
bash "$TRAIN" build t8 --no-fetch b19 >/dev/null 2>&1 || no "build t8"
git checkout -q train/t8
HEAD8="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"; MAIN8="$(git ls-remote origin refs/heads/main | cut -f1)"
bash "$TRAIN" check t8 --no-remote >/dev/null 2>&1 || no "check t8"
printf '[{"databaseId": 4343, "headSha": "%s", "createdAt": "9999-01-01T00:00:00Z"}]' "$HEAD8" > "$FAKE_GH_DIR/list.json"
touch "$FAKE_GH_DIR/drop.before"
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" ci t8 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" = 0 ] && has "$out" "retrying (1 of 3)" && [ "$(git ls-remote origin refs/heads/ci/t8 | cut -f1)" = "$HEAD8" ] \
  && ok "ci retries a push dropped before the remote moved" || { no "ci dropped push: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
runjson "$HEAD8" success success
touch "$FAKE_GH_DIR/drop.always"
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" land t8 2>&1)"; rc=$?
rm -f "$FAKE_GH_DIR/drop.always"
[ "$rc" != 0 ] && has "$out" "failed 3 time(s)" && [ "$(git ls-remote origin refs/heads/main | cut -f1)" = "$MAIN8" ] \
  && [ ! -d "$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)/concrete-push-both.lock" ] \
  && ok "exhausted retries fail the landing, move nothing and release the lock" || { no "exhausted retries: exit $rc"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; }
touch "$FAKE_GH_DIR/drop.before" "$FAKE_GH_DIR/drop.after"
out="$(bash "$TRAIN" land t8 2>&1)"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" = 0 ] && has "$out" "retrying (1 of 3)" && has "$out" "the connection dropped after the update" \

 succeeded in 0ms:
  && [ "$(git ls-remote origin refs/heads/main | cut -f1)" = "$HEAD8" ] \
