#!/usr/bin/env bash
# THE IMPACT GATES ON THE GATE HOST. `train.sh check`'s impact leg, run on the CI server (concrete-ci-2)
# instead of the machine that pushes: credentials stay on the pushing machine, the compute moves.
#
#   gates_remote.sh --host H --base <sha> --head <sha> [--src-ref R] [--dst-ref R]
#
# 1. Bundles base..head (a temporary ref, refs/land-remote/<id>, at head) and hands it to the
#    TRANSPORT, which runs scripts/land/run-at-ref.sh on the host: a fresh clone of exactly head,
#    where scripts/land/impact_gates.sh runs the same hook with the same input line the `ci` push will
#    present. The CONCRETE_* variables of this environment are forwarded, so the selection and the
#    record's environment component are the push's.
# 2. Reads the log. The run counts only when run-at-ref printed exactly one start and one exit line
#    and both name HEAD's SHA. A run at any other SHA, a lost connection, an unreachable host or a log
#    without the exit line is NO RESULT.
# 3. On a pass, keeps the log and writes the remote attestation (scripts/checks/lib/prepush_record.sh)
#    so the `ci` push's hook can reuse this run instead of running the gates on this machine. A pass
#    whose host wrote no record still passes the leg, and says that the push will run the gates here.
#
# Exit: 0 passed at head; 1 the gates FAILED at head; 3 INCOMPLETE (no result about head). The
# caller treats 3 as blocking; it never counts as a pass.
#
# Transport: LAND_GATES_TRANSPORT, called as `<transport> <host> <bundle> <ref> <command>`, must
# print run-at-ref.sh's output. The default, gates_transport_ssh.sh, copies the bundle to the host
# and pipes this checkout's run-at-ref.sh through ssh. The landing gate passes a fake.
set -uo pipefail
SELF_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
incomplete() { echo "gates-remote: INCOMPLETE: $*" >&2; exit 3; }
host="" base="" head="" src="refs/heads/impact" dst="refs/heads/ci/impact"
while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do
  case "$1" in
    --host) host="$2"; shift ;;
    --base) base="$2"; shift ;;
    --head) head="$2"; shift ;;
    --src-ref) src="$2"; shift ;;
    --dst-ref) dst="$2"; shift ;;
    *) incomplete "unknown argument $1" ;;
  esac; shift
done
[ -n "$host" ] && [ -n "$base" ] && [ -n "$head" ] || incomplete "--host, --base and --head are required"
case "$dst" in refs/heads/wip/*) incomplete "--dst-ref $dst is in the hook's wip tier, which runs no gates" ;; esac
ROOT="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)" || incomplete "not in a git checkout"
cd "$ROOT" || incomplete "cannot enter $ROOT"
head="$(git rev-parse --verify "$head^{commit}" 2>/dev/null)" || incomplete "--head is not a commit"
base="$(git rev-parse --verify "$base^{commit}" 2>/dev/null)" || incomplete "--base is not a commit"
[ "$(git rev-parse HEAD)" = "$head" ] || incomplete "the checkout is not at --head ${head:0:10}"
# The record library of the checkout this script belongs to (the train checkout, in practice).
# shellcheck source=scripts/checks/lib/prepush_record.sh
source "$SELF_DIR/../checks/lib/prepush_record.sh" 2>/dev/null && ts_require 2>/dev/null \
  || incomplete "scripts/checks/lib/prepush_record.sh did not load"
TRANSPORT="${LAND_GATES_TRANSPORT:-$SELF_DIR/gates_transport_ssh.sh}"
[ -x "$TRANSPORT" ] || [ -f "$TRANSPORT" ] || incomplete "no transport at $TRANSPORT"

TMPD="$(mktemp -d)" || incomplete "cannot create a temp directory"
id="$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%S)-$$-$RANDOM"
ref="refs/land-remote/$id"
cleanup() { git update-ref -d "$ref" 2>/dev/null || true; rm -rf "$TMPD"; }
trap cleanup EXIT
# This run replaces any earlier attestation: whatever it ends in, no older result stands beside it.
rm -f "$(ppr_remote_path "$ROOT")" "$(ppr_remote_log_path "$ROOT")" 2>/dev/null || true
git update-ref "$ref" "$head" || incomplete "cannot create $ref"
# base..head only: the host's clone already has base (it is on main). A host that lacks it cannot
# fetch the bundle, which is a transport failure (INCOMPLETE), never a pass.
if [ "$base" = "$head" ]; then range=("$ref"); else range=("$ref" "^$base"); fi
git bundle create "$TMPD/candidate.bundle" "${range[@]}" >/dev/null 2>&1 \
  || incomplete "git bundle create failed"

# The command run in the host's clone. It first clears the gate bypass and the hook's re-entry
# bookkeeping, whatever the host's environment holds: a hook that skips its gates exits 0, and a
# skipped run must never read as a pass. Then this environment's CONCRETE_* variables are forwarded:
# NUL-delimited (a value may hold newlines), names validated, values quoted with %q.
fwd=""
while IFS= read -r -d '' kv; do
  k="${kv%%=*}"; v="${kv#*=}"
  [[ "$k" =~ ^CONCRETE_[A-Za-z0-9_]+$ ]] || continue
  case "$k" in CONCRETE_SKIP_GATES|CONCRETE_GATES_SHELL|CONCRETE_PREPUSH_REENTERED) continue ;; esac
  fwd="$fwd export $k=$(printf '%q' "$v");"
done < <(env -0 | LC_ALL=C sort -z)
q() { printf '%q' "$1"; }
cmd="unset CONCRETE_SKIP_GATES CONCRETE_PREPUSH_REENTERED;$fwd bash scripts/land/impact_gates.sh --base $(q "$base") --head $(q "$head") --src-ref $(q "$src") --dst-ref $(q "$dst")"
cmd="$cmd && { r=\"\$(git rev-parse --git-path concrete-prepush-record)\"; if [ -f \"\$r\" ]; then echo $(q "$PPR_REMOTE_BEGIN"); cat \"\$r\"; echo $(q "$PPR_REMOTE_END"); else echo '=== concrete-remote-record-none'; fi; }"

LOG="$TMPD/remote.log"
echo "gates-remote: running the impact gates for ${base:0:10}..${head:0:10} on $host"
rc=0
"$TRANSPORT" "$host" "$TMPD/candidate.bundle" "$ref" "$cmd" > "$LOG" 2>&1 || rc=$?
cat "$LOG"

# The verdict comes from the log, bound to the SHA, never from the transport's exit status alone.
starts="$(grep -c '^\[concrete-remote\] ref=' "$LOG")"
exits="$(grep -c '^\[concrete-remote\] exit=' "$LOG")"
[ "$starts" = 1 ] && [ "$exits" = 1 ] \
  || incomplete "the host's log has $starts start and $exits exit line(s) (transport exit $rc); no result about ${head:0:10}"
grep -qE "^\[concrete-remote\] ref=[^ ]+ sha=$head( |\$)" "$LOG" \
  || incomplete "the host ran another commit than ${head:0:10}: $(grep '^\[concrete-remote\] ref=' "$LOG")"
grep -qE "^\[concrete-remote\] exit=[0-9]+ .*sha=$head\$" "$LOG" \
  || incomplete "the host's exit line does not name ${head:0:10}: $(grep '^\[concrete-remote\] exit=' "$LOG")"
if ! grep -qE "^\[concrete-remote\] exit=0 " "$LOG"; then
  echo "gates-remote: the impact gates FAILED on $host at ${head:0:10}" >&2
  exit 1
fi
if ppr_remote_attest "$ROOT" "$LOG" "$host" "$head"; then
  echo "gates-remote: passed on $host at ${head:0:10}; attestation $(ppr_remote_path "$ROOT") (the ci push reuses it)"
else
  echo "gates-remote: passed on $host at ${head:0:10}, but no attestation ($PPR_REASON): the ci push will run the gates on this machine"
fi
echo "impact: gates passed for ${base:0:10}..${head:0:10} on $host"
exit 0
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# The default transport of scripts/land/gates_remote.sh:
#
#   gates_transport_ssh.sh <host> <bundle> <ref> <command>
#
# Copies ONE directory to <host> (any ssh destination, e.g. an ~/.ssh/config alias): the bundle, this
# checkout's scripts/land/run-at-ref.sh with the sandbox helper it sources (scripts/checks/lib/
# git_sandbox.sh, staged at the same relative paths), and a bash script that brings the host's gate clone up to
# date with its origin (so base, which is on main, is there) and runs run-at-ref.sh with
# RUN_AT_REF_SOURCE=<the bundle>, <ref> and <command>. The ref and command are quoted for bash HERE,
# into that file, so the host's login shell only ever sees `bash land-remote-<id>/run.sh`, whatever
# the command contains. Output is run-at-ref.sh's. The login must not be root. The host needs its gate clone (RUN_AT_REF_REPO,
# default ~/concrete2) and nix; it needs no GitHub credentials and no copy of the landing scripts.
#
# Env: LAND_GATES_SSH (default `ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ServerAliveInterval=20 -o ServerAliveCountMax=120`),
#      LAND_GATES_SCP (default `scp -q -r -o BatchMode=yes`; must copy a directory).
set -uo pipefail
[ $# = 4 ] || { echo "usage: gates_transport_ssh.sh <host> <bundle> <ref> <command>" >&2; exit 2; }
host="$1" bundle="$2" ref="$3" cmd="$4"
SELF_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
read -r -a SSH <<< "${LAND_GATES_SSH:-ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ServerAliveInterval=20 -o ServerAliveCountMax=120}"
read -r -a SCP <<< "${LAND_GATES_SCP:-scp -q -r -o BatchMode=yes}"
id="land-remote-$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%S)-$$-$RANDOM"
tmp="$(mktemp -d)" || { echo "transport: cannot create a temp directory" >&2; exit 255; }
trap 'rm -rf "$tmp"' EXIT
mkdir -p "$tmp/$id/scripts/land" "$tmp/$id/scripts/checks/lib"
cp "$bundle" "$tmp/$id/c.bundle" && cp "$SELF_DIR/run-at-ref.sh" "$tmp/$id/scripts/land/run-at-ref.sh" \
  && cp "$SELF_DIR/../checks/lib/git_sandbox.sh" "$tmp/$id/scripts/checks/lib/git_sandbox.sh" \
  || { echo "transport: cannot stage the bundle" >&2; exit 255; }
{
  echo '#!/usr/bin/env bash'
  echo 'dir="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"'
  echo 'trap '\''rm -rf "$dir"'\'' EXIT'
  echo 'gc="${RUN_AT_REF_REPO:-$HOME/concrete2}"'
  # Who the ssh destination logged in as, checked ON THE HOST before anything is fetched: gates never
  # run as root, an unreadable identity is not "not root", and a login without its gate clone names
  # the account and path. (2026-11-08: a train run from an account whose `concrete-ci-2` alias is
  # the root admin login looked for /root/concrete2 and reported only "fetch in the gate clone
  # failed".) Each is a transport failure: INCOMPLETE, never a pass.
  echo 'uid="$(id -u 2>/dev/null)"; who="$(id -un 2>/dev/null)@$(hostname 2>/dev/null)"'
  echo 'case "$uid" in ""|*[!0-9]*) echo "transport: cannot read the gate host login (id -u gave \"$uid\")" >&2; exit 255 ;; esac'
  echo '[ "$uid" != 0 ] || { echo "transport: the gate host login is $who (root); gates run as the gate user (dev on the CI hosts), never root: check the ssh destination User" >&2; exit 255; }'
  # A gate clone is a work tree whose top level is $gc (a clone, or a worktree whose .git is a file).
  echo 'top="$(git -C "$gc" rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null)"'
  echo '[ -n "$top" ] && [ "$(cd "$gc" && pwd -P)" = "$(cd "$top" && pwd -P)" ] || { echo "transport: no gate clone at $gc for $who (RUN_AT_REF_REPO); is the ssh destination the gate user?" >&2; exit 255; }'
  echo 'git -C "$gc" fetch -q origin || { echo "transport: fetch in the gate clone failed" >&2; exit 255; }'
  printf 'RUN_AT_REF_SOURCE="$dir/c.bundle" bash "$dir/scripts/land/run-at-ref.sh" %q %q\n' "$ref" "$cmd"
} > "$tmp/$id/run.sh"
"${SCP[@]}" "$tmp/$id" "$host:$id" || { echo "transport: copying to $host failed" >&2; exit 255; }
rc=0
"${SSH[@]}" "$host" "bash $id/run.sh" < /dev/null || rc=$?
exit "$rc"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# run-at-ref.sh <ref-or-sha> <command...>
# The server-local twin of the laptop's ~/bin/concrete-remote, run ON the gate host (by
# scripts/land/gates_remote.sh): fetch <ref> into the gate clone, run <command> in a fresh CLONE of it at that commit,
# inside `nix develop`, print `[concrete-remote] ... sha=<sha>` lines, exit with the command's status.
#
# A clone, not a worktree: some gates refuse to run in a linked worktree (the mutation campaign and
# its summary producer, check_phase6c_observability #5: "Run it in a clone"), so a full set run in
# a worktree is red for a reason that has nothing to do with the candidate. `clone --local`
# hardlinks the objects, so it costs about what a worktree costs.
#
# Env: RUN_AT_REF_REPO (gate clone, default ~/concrete2), RUN_AT_REF_RUNS (default ~/runs),
#      RUN_AT_REF_SOURCE (what to fetch from: a remote name, a path to a clone or a git bundle,
#      default origin; scripts/land/gates_remote.sh passes a bundle so an unpublished train SHA can
#      run), RUN_AT_REF_KEEP=1, RUN_AT_REF_NO_NIX=1 (run the command
#      without `nix develop`: the landing gate's sandbox only).
set -euo pipefail
# The clone below is a sandbox: no repository variable of the caller may reach it (ISS-0015). The
# transport stages the helper beside this script at the same relative path.
source "$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)/../checks/lib/git_sandbox.sh"
git_sandbox
if [ $# -lt 2 ]; then sed -n '2,16p' "$0"; exit 2; fi
REF="$1"; shift
CMD="$*"
REPO="${RUN_AT_REF_REPO:-$HOME/concrete2}"
RUNS="${RUN_AT_REF_RUNS:-$HOME/runs}"
SRC="${RUN_AT_REF_SOURCE:-origin}"
mkdir -p "$RUNS"
ID="$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%S)-$$"; WT="$RUNS/$ID"
cleanup() {
  if [ "${RUN_AT_REF_KEEP:-0}" = 1 ]; then echo "[concrete-remote] kept clone: $WT"; return 0; fi
  rm -rf "$WT"
}
{
  flock 9
  git -C "$REPO" fetch --quiet "$SRC" "$REF"
  SHA="$(git -C "$REPO" rev-parse FETCH_HEAD)"
  git -C "$REPO" update-ref "refs/run-at-ref/$ID" "$SHA"   # keeps the commit reachable for the clone
} 9>"$RUNS/.fetch.lock"
trap 'cleanup; git -C "$REPO" update-ref -d "refs/run-at-ref/$ID" 2>/dev/null || true' EXIT
trap 'exit 130' INT TERM HUP
git clone --quiet --local --no-checkout "$REPO" "$WT"
git -C "$WT" fetch --quiet origin "+refs/remotes/origin/*:refs/remotes/origin/*" "+refs/run-at-ref/$ID:refs/run-at-ref/$ID"
git -C "$WT" checkout --quiet --detach "$SHA"
echo "[concrete-remote] ref=$REF sha=$SHA clone=$WT host=$(hostname)"
if [ -d "$REPO/.lake" ]; then cp -a "$REPO/.lake" "$WT/.lake"; fi
cd "$WT"
START=$(date +%s)
RC=0
if [ "${RUN_AT_REF_NO_NIX:-0}" = 1 ]; then bash -c "$CMD" || RC=$?
else nix develop -c bash -c "$CMD" || RC=$?; fi
echo "[concrete-remote] exit=$RC elapsed=$(( $(date +%s) - START ))s sha=$SHA"
exit "$RC"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# The impact-selected gates for exactly the range base..head, run in the current checkout (which must
# be at head). Shared by train.sh check and ready.sh.
#
#   impact_gates.sh --base <sha> --head <sha> [--src-ref R] [--dst-ref R] [--report FILE]
#
# 1. The pre-push hook's own selection (R-0022 fast surface gates + touched areas), fed the line
#    `<src-ref> <head> <dst-ref> <base>`, so a later push presenting that range reuses its record.
#    The dst ref must not be wip/* (the hook's backup tier runs no gates); default refs/heads/ci/impact.
# 2. scripts/tests/select_gates.sh --base --head (R-0513), when it exists and the hook does not
#    already call it, run through run_ci_gates_local.sh --first.
# --report FILE receives `selected: N` (the select_gates count, or `hook` when only the hook ran).
# LAND_HOOK, LAND_REMOTE, LAND_NO_NIX as in train.sh.
set -euo pipefail
SELF_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
# shellcheck source=scripts/land/lib.sh
. "$SELF_DIR/lib.sh"
die() { echo "impact: $*" >&2; exit 1; }
base="" head="" src="refs/heads/impact" dst="refs/heads/ci/impact" report=""
while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do
  case "$1" in
    --base) base="$2"; shift ;;
    --head) head="$2"; shift ;;
    --src-ref) src="$2"; shift ;;
    --dst-ref) dst="$2"; shift ;;
    --report) report="$2"; shift ;;
    *) die "unknown argument $1" ;;
  esac; shift
done
[ -n "$base" ] && [ -n "$head" ] || die "--base and --head are required"
case "$dst" in refs/heads/wip/*) die "--dst-ref $dst is in the hook's wip tier, which runs no gates" ;; esac
cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"
HOOK="${LAND_HOOK:-.githooks/pre-push}"
REMOTE="${LAND_REMOTE:-origin}"
[ "$(git rev-parse HEAD)" = "$(git rev-parse "$head")" ] || die "the checkout is not at --head $head"
[ -f "$HOOK" ] || die "no pre-push hook at $HOOK"
url="$(git remote get-url "$REMOTE" 2>/dev/null || echo "$REMOTE")"
printf '%s %s %s %s\n' "$src" "$(git rev-parse "$head")" "$dst" "$(git rev-parse "$base")" \
  | land_in_nix bash "$HOOK" "$REMOTE" "$url"
selected=hook
if [ -f scripts/tests/select_gates.sh ] && ! grep -q select_gates "$HOOK" 2>/dev/null; then
  sel="$(land_in_nix bash scripts/tests/select_gates.sh --base "$base" --head "$head")"
  args=() c=""
  while IFS= read -r c; do [ -n "$c" ] && args+=(--first "$c"); done <<< "$sel"
  selected="$(( ${#args[@]} / 2 ))"
  if [ "${#args[@]}" -gt 0 ]; then land_in_nix bash scripts/checks/run.sh "${args[@]}"; fi
fi
[ -z "$report" ] || echo "selected: $selected" > "$report"
echo "impact: gates passed for ${base:0:10}..${head:0:10} (selected: $selected)"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# R-0544 — does a commit carry a clean review verdict? See docs/REVIEW.md.
#
#   scripts/review/verify.sh [--fetch] <commit>
#
# Reads the git note that scripts/review/review.sh wrote on <commit> under
# refs/notes/concrete-review. The verdict counts only for that exact commit: a
# clean note on an ancestor says nothing about the commits after it.
#
#   --fetch  first fetch refs/notes/concrete-review from $REVIEW_REMOTE (default
#            origin), replacing the local copy (a reader, not a writer).
#
# Exit codes: 0 clean; 1 reviewed, not clean (findings or blocked);
#             2 no note, a malformed note, or a usage error.

set -euo pipefail

NOTES_REF="refs/notes/concrete-review"
REMOTE="${REVIEW_REMOTE:-origin}"
FETCH=0
if [ "${1:-}" = --fetch ]; then FETCH=1; shift; fi
[ $# -eq 1 ] || { echo "usage: verify.sh [--fetch] <commit>" >&2; exit 2; }

if [ "$FETCH" = 1 ]; then
  git fetch -q "$REMOTE" "+$NOTES_REF:$NOTES_REF" \
    || { echo "verify: could not fetch $NOTES_REF from $REMOTE" >&2; exit 2; }
fi

SHA="$(git rev-parse -q --verify "$1^{commit}")" || { echo "verify: not a commit: $1" >&2; exit 2; }
NOTE="$(git notes --ref="$NOTES_REF" show "$SHA" 2>/dev/null)" || {
  echo "$SHA unreviewed: no note under $NOTES_REF"
  exit 2
}

# The note must carry each of the six fields review.sh writes exactly once, each well formed. Any
# other key, a repeated key, or a line that is not `key: value` makes the note malformed. Keys are
# matched by name, not position, because notes.sh `put` appends `utc` last.
malformed() {
  echo "$SHA malformed review note${1:+ ($1)}:"
  printf '%s\n' "$NOTE" | sed 's/^/  /'
  exit 2
}
# Plain variables F_<key>, not an associative array: macOS /bin/bash is 3.2.
SEEN=" "
while IFS= read -r line; do
  [[ "$line" =~ ^([a-z-]+):\ (.*)$ ]] || malformed "not key: value: '$line'"
  k="${BASH_REMATCH[1]}" v="${BASH_REMATCH[2]}"
  case "$SEEN" in *" $k "*) malformed "repeated key $k" ;; esac
  SEEN="$SEEN$k "
  printf -v "F_${k//-/_}" '%s' "$v"
  case "$k" in
    verdict)      [[ "$v" =~ ^(clean|findings|blocked)$ ]] ;;
    rounds)       [[ "$v" =~ ^[1-9][0-9]*$ ]] ;;
    reviewer)     [[ "$v" =~ ^[^[:space:]]+$ ]] ;;
    utc)          [[ "$v" =~ ^[0-9]{4}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2}T[0-9]{2}:[0-9]{2}:[0-9]{2}Z$ ]] ;;
    base)         [[ "$v" =~ ^[0-9a-f]{40}$ ]] ;;
    pieces)       [[ "$v" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] ;;
    *)            false ;;
  esac || malformed "bad $k"
done <<< "$NOTE"
for k in verdict rounds reviewer utc base pieces; do
  case "$SEEN" in *" $k "*) ;; *) malformed "missing $k" ;; esac
done

field() { local n="F_${1//-/_}"; printf '%s' "${!n:-}"; }
VERDICT="$(field verdict)"
echo "$SHA $VERDICT (round $(field rounds), reviewer $(field reviewer), $(field utc), base $(field base), $(field pieces) piece(s))"
[ "$VERDICT" = clean ] && exit 0
exit 1
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Pre-push gate for the restarted root.
#
# A push that can reach main or a ci/* candidate builds the specification and runs every root check
# (scripts/checks/run.sh), the same set CI's "Root checks" and "Spec build" jobs run. The set is small
# and fast, so there is no selection: everything runs on every such push. legacy/ is frozen and never
# run; scripts/checks/legacy_frozen.sh is the only check that looks at it.
#
# Tiers decided from git's ref lines on stdin (`<local ref> <local sha> <remote ref> <remote sha>`):
#   - only refs/heads/wip/* updates or deletions: a backup of unfinished work, which can never become
#     main. Conflict markers are refused; nothing else runs.
#   - only the review-note refs (refs/notes/concrete-review, refs/notes/concrete-review-findings,
#     written by scripts/review/review.sh) and owner sign-off records (refs/owner-signoff/<sha>,
#     written by scripts/land/signoff.sh): no source tree, nothing to check.
#   - anything else, including a by-hand run with no stdin: the full root set, and only from a full
#     clone (a linked worktree is refused; ISS-0015).
#
# Deliberate bypass:  CONCRETE_SKIP_GATES=1 git push   (you then own the CI result)
set -uo pipefail
HOOK_SELF="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)/$(basename "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")"
# train.sh asks the hook git would run which hook it is (ISS-0015): called with the single argument
# --concrete-identify (git always passes a remote name and URL, never this), this one answers with
# its real path and runs nothing. A wrapper that passes its arguments on to it (acceptance.sh's
# guard) gets the same answer; a hook that never runs this one gives none. An argument, not an
# environment variable, so nothing a push inherits can turn the gates off.
if [ "$#" = 1 ] && [ "$1" = --concrete-identify ]; then
  echo "concrete-pre-push $(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd -P)/$(basename "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")"
  exit 0
fi
ROOT_DIR="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"
cd "$ROOT_DIR"

if [ "${CONCRETE_SKIP_GATES:-0}" = "1" ]; then
  echo "pre-push: gates SKIPPED by CONCRETE_SKIP_GATES=1 — you own the CI result." >&2
  exit 0
fi

wip_refs=0; wip_dels=0; note_refs=0; other_refs=0; wip_shas=""; full_shas=""
# Kept whole so the hook can hand the same lines to itself when it re-enters the gate shell (below).
HOOK_STDIN="$(cat)"
while read -r _localref localsha remoteref _remotesha; do
  [ -z "${localsha:-}" ] && continue
  case "${remoteref:-}" in
    refs/heads/wip/*)
      case "$localsha" in
        *[!0]*) wip_refs=$((wip_refs+1)); wip_shas="$wip_shas $localsha" ;;
        *) wip_dels=$((wip_dels+1)) ;;
      esac ;;
    refs/owner-signoff/*)
      # Only a record takes the notes tier: a parentless commit, under its subject's 40-hex name, whose
      # tree is exactly the sign-off's seven plain files (record.txt plus each reviewer's request and
      # output), no directory among them. Anything else pushed here, including the old four-file shape
      # or a source commit, takes the full tier, as does a deletion.
      if [[ "${remoteref#refs/owner-signoff/}" =~ ^[0-9a-f]{40}$ ]] && [ "$(git rev-list --parents -n 1 "$localsha" 2>/dev/null)" = "$localsha" ] \
         && [ "$(git ls-tree --format='%(objectmode) %(objecttype) %(path)' "$localsha" 2>/dev/null | LC_ALL=C sort | tr '\n' ' ')" \
              = "100644 blob agy-request.txt 100644 blob agy.txt 100644 blob codex-request.txt 100644 blob codex.txt 100644 blob kimi-request.txt 100644 blob kimi.txt 100644 blob record.txt " ]; then
        note_refs=$((note_refs+1))
      else
        other_refs=$((other_refs+1))
        case "$localsha" in *[!0]*) full_shas="$full_shas $localsha" ;; esac
      fi ;;
    refs/notes/concrete-review|refs/notes/concrete-review-findings)
      case "$localsha" in
        *[!0]*) note_refs=$((note_refs+1)) ;;
        *) other_refs=$((other_refs+1)) ;;   # deleting a review note takes the full tier
      esac ;;
    *) other_refs=$((other_refs+1))
       case "$localsha" in *[!0]*) full_shas="$full_shas $localsha" ;; esac ;;
  esac
done <<< "$HOOK_STDIN"

if [ "$other_refs" -eq 0 ] && [ $((wip_refs + wip_dels)) -gt 0 ]; then
  bad=""
  for sha in $wip_shas; do
    rc=0; git grep -qE '^(<{7}|>{7}|\|{7})( |$)|^={7}$' "$sha" -- . ':(exclude)legacy/' >/dev/null 2>&1 || rc=$?
    case "$rc" in
      0) bad="$bad $sha" ;;
      1) ;;
      *) echo "pre-push (wip tier): could not scan $sha (git grep exit $rc) — refusing"; exit 1 ;;
    esac
  done
  [ -z "$bad" ] || { echo "pre-push (wip tier): conflict markers in$bad — refusing"; exit 1; }
  echo "pre-push: wip/* backup push ($wip_refs update(s), $wip_dels deletion(s)) — conflict-marker check only; the full set runs on ci/* and main"
  exit 0
fi
if [ "$other_refs" -eq 0 ] && [ "$note_refs" -gt 0 ]; then
  echo "pre-push: review-notes push (review notes, owner sign-offs) — no source tree, no gates"
  exit 0
fi

# The checks build sandbox repositories, and a linked worktree hands its hook
# GIT_DIR=<repo>/.git/worktrees/<name>: on 2026-11-06 that turned a review-tool check into writes on a
# real clone and on GitHub (ISS-0015). Every repository-making script now clears git's variables, and
# as a second wall a push that runs the checks must come from a full clone. The wip and notes tiers
# above run nothing, so a worktree may still back up its branch.
if [ "$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-dir)" && pwd -P)" != "$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)" && pwd -P)" ]; then
  echo "pre-push: refusing: this push runs the root checks, and $ROOT_DIR is a linked worktree; push it from a full clone (git clone), never a worktree." >&2
  exit 1
fi

# The checks run in the gate shell (scripts/checks/lib/gate_env.sh refuses anywhere else). From
# outside it, the hook re-executes itself inside `nix develop` with the same arguments and stdin.
if [ "${CONCRETE_GATES_SHELL:-}" != 1 ] || ! command -v lake >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  if [ "${CONCRETE_PREPUSH_REENTERED:-0}" = 1 ]; then
    echo "pre-push: nix develop did not provide the gate shell with Lean — refusing to push." >&2; exit 1
  fi
  command -v nix >/dev/null 2>&1 || {
    echo "pre-push: the checks run inside nix develop, and nix is not on PATH — refusing to push." >&2; exit 1; }
  echo "pre-push: entering nix develop for the checks"
  CONCRETE_PREPUSH_REENTERED=1 exec nix develop "$ROOT_DIR" --command bash "$HOOK_SELF" "$@" <<< "$HOOK_STDIN"
fi

# The checks judge the work tree, so it must be the commit being pushed, unmodified.
head="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"
for sha in $full_shas; do
  [ "$sha" = "$head" ] || {
    echo "pre-push: pushing ${sha:0:10}, but the checkout is at ${head:0:10}; the checks would judge the wrong commit — check it out and push from there." >&2
    exit 1; }
done
if [ -n "$(git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no)" ]; then
  echo "pre-push: the work tree has uncommitted changes to tracked files; the checks would not judge what is pushed — refusing." >&2
  exit 1
fi

echo "pre-push: building the specification…"
BUILD_LOG="$(mktemp "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/prepush-build.XXXXXX")" || BUILD_LOG=/dev/null
lake build >"$BUILD_LOG" 2>&1 || { echo "pre-push: BUILD FAILED — see $BUILD_LOG" >&2; exit 1; }

echo "pre-push: root checks…"
bash scripts/checks/run.sh || { echo "pre-push: ROOT CHECKS FAILED (above) — fix, or bypass deliberately." >&2; exit 1; }
echo "pre-push: build and root checks pass"
exit 0
# Required CI jobs: a train lands only when every job named here ran at the candidate's exact SHA and
# succeeded (scripts/land/train.sh watch and land). One exact job name per line, as GitHub shows it.
# GitHub branch protection is not configured for main, so this file is the record. Every job of
# .github/workflows/lean_action_ci.yml is listed, each matrix leg under the name GitHub shows for it:

name: CI

# The root's CI after the legacy/ move. Eighteen jobs, all required (required-checks.txt):
#   Landing record  main's push run names the run that qualified its exact SHA (R-0570), kept from
#                the base unchanged; when it finds one, the jobs below are skipped on main.
#   Root checks  every check of scripts/checks/run.sh: legacy/ frozen, the day-one root inventory,
#                workflow YAML, Markdown links, the landing script and readiness, the review tool.
#   Spec build   lake build of the specification, warnings refused.
#   Proof axioms, Proof trust ban (sorry, native_decide), Kernel replay (leanchecker), External
#                re-check (con-leche, nanoda)  the proof-integrity checks of scripts/proofs.
#   Slice 0 backends, artifacts, trusted base, reproducibility (x86-64 Linux and AArch64 macOS)
#                the compiler's gates (scripts/checks/compiler), each in the compiler's devshell.
#   Oracle (GitHub-hosted fetch + smoke)  the published oracle fetched, verified and run on a
#                GitHub-hosted runner (ISS-0005, P13; R-0564), kept from the base unchanged.
# legacy/ is never built or run here: its gates were qualified at tag legacy-compiler-final. The old
# workflows are archived, unrun, under legacy/.github/workflows.
# A train's dispatch names its base: the run's title then says which base it was built on, and
# main's Landing record accepts a run only for the base main had before the push (R-0570).
run-name: ${{ github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch' && inputs.base != '' && format('{0} at base {1}', github.ref_name, inputs.base) || '' }}

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
  pull_request:
  workflow_dispatch:
    inputs:
      base:
        # THE BASE OF A TRAIN'S CANDIDATE, passed by train.sh ci (-f base=<sha>). The root has no
        # base-sensitive check yet; the title records the base so main's Landing record can require
        # that the run it accepts was built on the base main had before the landing.
        description: "Base SHA of a train's candidate (recorded in the run title)"
        type: string
        required: false
        default: ""

concurrency:
  group: ci-${{ github.ref }}-${{ github.event_name }}
  cancel-in-progress: ${{ github.event_name == 'push' || github.event_name == 'pull_request' }}

# Every run step executes in the gate userland (flake.nix, `gates` shell), which marks itself with
# CONCRETE_GATES_SHELL=1; scripts/checks/lib/gate_env.sh refuses anywhere else. It contains no Lean:
# lean-action's elan toolchain is the one on PATH in the spec job.
defaults:
  run:
    shell: "nix develop .#gates --command bash --noprofile --norc -eo pipefail {0}"

jobs:
  # MAIN'S RUN NAMES THE RUN THAT QUALIFIED ITS EXACT SHA (R-0570), as in the base's workflow. On a
  # push to main this job looks up, with train.sh record, a completed dispatch of this workflow at
  # exactly this SHA and at the base main had before the push, with every required job succeeded.
  # Found: every other job is skipped and the run links it. Not found, or any other event: every job
  # runs. Each job below needs it and carries the gate; check_landing_script.sh holds both.
  landing-record:
    name: Landing record
    runs-on: ${{ (github.event_name != 'pull_request' && vars.CONCRETE_SELF_HOSTED == 'true') && fromJSON('["self-hosted","linux","x64","concrete"]') || 'ubuntu-latest' }}
    timeout-minutes: 15
    permissions:
      actions: read
      contents: read
    outputs:
      recorded: ${{ steps.record.outputs.recorded }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
        if: github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'

      - name: Install Nix (GitHub-hosted only)
        if: github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' && runner.environment != 'self-hosted'
        uses: cachix/install-nix-action@v31
        with:
          extra_nix_config: |
            experimental-features = nix-command flakes

      - name: "Look up the run that qualified this exact SHA (none found: every job runs here)"
        id: record
        if: github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'
        env:
          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
          BEFORE: ${{ github.event.before }}
          HEAD_SHA: ${{ github.sha }}
          LAND_REPO: ${{ github.repository }}
          LAND_NO_NIX: "1"
        run: |
          recorded=false run=""
          if out="$(git fetch -q --depth=1 origin "$BEFORE" 2>&1 \
                    && bash scripts/land/train.sh record "$HEAD_SHA" --base "$BEFORE" --exclude-run "$GITHUB_RUN_ID" 2>&1)"; then
            run="$(printf '%s\n' "$out" | sed -n 's/^qualifying_run=\([0-9][0-9]*\)$/\1/p' | tail -1)"
          fi
          printf '%s\n' "$out"
          if [ -n "$run" ]; then
            recorded=true
            { echo "### Landing record"
              echo "Run [$run](https://github.com/$LAND_REPO/actions/runs/$run) passed every landing-tier and required job at exactly \`$HEAD_SHA\`."
              echo "This run skips those jobs: that run is the result for this SHA."; } >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
          else
            { echo "### No landing record"
              echo "No run qualifies \`$HEAD_SHA\`, so every job runs here:"
              printf '\n```\n%s\n```\n' "$out"; } >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
          fi
          echo "recorded=$recorded" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"

  root-checks:
    name: Root checks
    needs: landing-record
    if: ${{ !cancelled() && needs.landing-record.outputs.recorded != 'true' }}
    # Self-hosted runners never take pull_request events (forks could run code on them).
    runs-on: ${{ (github.event_name != 'pull_request' && vars.CONCRETE_SELF_HOSTED == 'true') && fromJSON('["self-hosted","linux","x64","concrete"]') || 'ubuntu-latest' }}
    steps:
      # Full history: legacy_frozen.sh reads the move commit named in scripts/checks/legacy.lock.
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0

      - name: Install Nix (GitHub-hosted only)
        if: runner.environment != 'self-hosted'
        uses: cachix/install-nix-action@v31
        with:
          extra_nix_config: |
            experimental-features = nix-command flakes

      - name: Root checks (scripts/checks/run.sh)
        run: bash scripts/checks/run.sh

  spec:
    name: Spec build
    needs: landing-record
    if: ${{ !cancelled() && needs.landing-record.outputs.recorded != 'true' }}
    runs-on: ${{ (github.event_name != 'pull_request' && vars.CONCRETE_SELF_HOSTED == 'true') && fromJSON('["self-hosted","linux","x64","concrete"]') || 'ubuntu-latest' }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Install Nix (GitHub-hosted only)
        if: runner.environment != 'self-hosted'
        uses: cachix/install-nix-action@v31
        with:
          extra_nix_config: |
            experimental-features = nix-command flakes

      - name: Install Lean (via lean-action)
        id: lean_install
        # A toolchain download can fail on a dropped connection; one paused retry keeps a network
        # blip from failing (and hiding) the whole run.
        continue-on-error: true
        uses: leanprover/lean-action@v1
        with:
          build-args: "--log-level=warning"

      - name: Wait before retrying the Lean install
        if: steps.lean_install.outcome == 'failure'
        run: sleep 60

      - name: Install Lean (via lean-action) (retry)
        if: steps.lean_install.outcome == 'failure'
        uses: leanprover/lean-action@v1
        with:
          build-args: "--log-level=warning"

      - name: Build the specification, warnings refused
        run: |
          OUTPUT=$(lake build 2>&1) || { echo "$OUTPUT"; exit 1; }
          if printf '%s\n' "$OUTPUT" | grep -i "warning"; then
            echo "::error::Build produced warnings"
            printf '%s\n' "$OUTPUT"
            exit 1
          fi

  # lean4export, con-leche and nanoda are built once per pinned revision (scripts/proofs/external.pins)

  # The oracle check on a GitHub-hosted runner (ISS-0005, P13; R-0564): proves that a runner other than
  # concrete-ci-2 gets a verified oracle from the release alone. A missing, corrupt or unverifiable
  # artifact makes ci_check.sh exit 3 (INCOMPLETE) and the job fail: it is never reported as passed.

  # THE COMPILER'S GATES (Concrete/README.md gates 4, 6, 6a, 8, 9, 10, 11 and 12; slice 0, provisional),
  # scripts/checks/compiler/: not root checks (run.sh runs only scripts/checks/*.sh), because they need
  # the compiler's devshell, a native macOS runner and minutes each. Every hand-written BIR example
  # through the LLVM path and the LCB path, each executable's observation compared with the BIR
  # semantics; sanitizers, binary size (report-only) and the stack bound; the trusted-base golden; and
  # every build stage reproduced in distinct directories and on distinct machines. Each runs natively
  # on x86-64 Linux and on AArch64 macOS (LCB's first native proof profile is Apple AArch64), and every
  # matrix leg is a required job of its own name.
  backends:
    name: Slice 0 backends (${{ matrix.arch }})
    needs: landing-record
    if: ${{ !cancelled() && needs.landing-record.outputs.recorded != 'true' }}
    # The compiler's devshell (flake.nix default: Lean, clang, LLVM, gdb/lldb), entered per step;

`self-hosted`, `linux` and `x64` are added automatically, and `--labels concrete` adds the fourth
label the workflow asks for.

### State between jobs (persistent runners)

A GitHub-hosted job starts on a fresh VM. A self-hosted one does not. Here is what the workflow
already handles, and what the host has to handle.

- **Workspace.** `actions/checkout@v4` (default `clean: true`) runs `git clean -ffdx` and
  `git reset --hard` on the existing checkout. That removes `.lake/`, `.mutation-evidence/`,
  leftovers of a cancelled mutation shard and any tracked file a gate mutated, before the cache
  restores `.lake/build`. The proof replay's "no build caches" property holds for the same
  reason.
- **`$RUNNER_TEMP`** is emptied by the runner for every job. The workflow's own temporary files
  live there: the LL(1) checker binaries, the trust-gate log, the thesis-demo reports, the example
  outputs, the checker acceptance evidence and the mutation shard records.
- **`/tmp` is NOT cleaned, and many gates write fixed `/tmp/...` paths.** Two runners on one host
  would overwrite each other's files, and a stale file from an earlier job could be read by a later
  one. Give every runner service a private `/tmp`, and empty it when each job starts:

  ```sh
  # Private /tmp per runner service (systemd unit created by svc.sh)
  sudo systemctl edit "$(cat /home/runner1/actions-runner/.service)"
  #   [Service]
  #   PrivateTmp=true
  sudo systemctl restart "$(cat /home/runner1/actions-runner/.service)"
  ```

  ```sh
  # /home/runner1/job-started.sh  (chmod +x), wired up in the runner's .env file:
  #   ACTIONS_RUNNER_HOOK_JOB_STARTED=/home/runner1/job-started.sh
  #!/bin/sh
  # /tmp is private to this runner service (PrivateTmp), so emptying it touches no other process.
  find /tmp -mindepth 1 -maxdepth 1 -user "$(id -u)" -exec rm -rf {} + 2>/dev/null || true
  ```

  An ephemeral runner in a fresh container or VM needs neither.
- **`$HOME` caches** persist: `~/.elan` (the toolchain, keyed by `lean-toolchain`) and
  `~/.cache/concrete-ext`. In that cache, each tool is built into a directory named by its pinned
  SHA and checked against it, and lean4export, which is rebuilt with this repository's toolchain,
  also has that toolchain in its directory name. So a toolchain bump builds a new exporter instead of
  reusing one that cannot read the new `.olean` files. Both caches are keyed by everything their
  builds depend on, so keeping them between jobs makes runs faster without affecting correctness.
  They live in the user's HOME, so each runner has its own. Old directories are never deleted; prune
  `~/.cache/concrete-ext` now and then.
- **Processes.** The runner kills a job's process tree when the job ends. A gate that leaves a
  daemon behind would survive on a persistent runner. That is one more reason to prefer ephemeral
  runners.

### Checking it works

1. Settings, Actions, Runners lists each runner as Idle, with labels
   `self-hosted, Linux, X64, concrete`.
2. `gh variable set CONCRETE_SELF_HOSTED --body true -R lambdaclass/concrete`.
3. `gh workflow run CI --ref main -R lambdaclass/concrete` (landing tier only), then
   check in the run's job pages that "Runner name" is one of yours and that `Install clang` printed
   `self-hosted runner, using preinstalled clang`.
4. To turn it off, delete the variable. Queued jobs that are still waiting for a self-hosted
   runner have to be cancelled and re-run.

## Build cache (R-0546)

Every job, worktree and agent used to build the compiler from nothing. Two caches now start that
build warm. **Neither one skips it.** `lake build` still runs and re-checks every module's trace,
and the BuildIdentity freshness gate still runs.

### Lean: `.lake/build` seeds

`scripts/dev/seed-lake.sh` copies an existing `.lake/build` into a checkout that has none. It
**refuses** (exit 3, nothing copied) unless the source matches on the **seed key**:

- the platform (`uname -s`/`-m`);
- `lean-toolchain`;
- the sha256 of `lake-manifest.json`;
- the sha256 of the **lean executable itself**.

The executable is in the key because lake's traces record only the Lean version and commit. Elan's
official archive and the devshell's autoPatchelf'd Linux copy would otherwise accept each other's
`.olean` files, and a module kernel-checked by one binary would be credited to the other (R-0208).
For a checkout, the script reads the binary from the checkout's own lake traces. Traces from other
Lean versions are ignored, because lake rebuilds those modules anyway.

Sources and `lakefile.toml` are not in the key. A seed from other sources is the useful case, and
lake mixes each module's sources and options into its trace. A source hash only chooses between
cache entries.

| Where | Seed source | How |
|---|---|---|
| Self-hosted CI runner | `/var/cache/concrete-lake/<key>/{<srchash>,latest}` | workflow step `Seed .lake from the server build cache (self-hosted only)` |
| GitHub-hosted runner | `actions/cache` (`.lake/build`, exact source key) and lean-action's `.lake` cache (previous commit) | unchanged |
| New worktree (laptop or server) | the main checkout's `.lake/build` | `bash scripts/dev/seed-lake.sh` (inside `nix develop`), then `lake build` |

- **Every same-version module must name its executable.** A trace from this Lean version that
  does not name the binary that compiled it refuses the whole seed, because it may be a fetched
are not a signature against someone who can write the git directory.

## Where the record lives (R-0547)

Every subcommand appends one row to the train's log:

- the time in UTC;
- the train, stage, base and head;
- the members, as `ref@sha`;
- the run id, result and duration;
- a note.

Results use the promotion pipeline's vocabulary:

- `passed`, `failed` and `skipped`;
- `incomplete` for no verdict;
- `refused` and `conflict` for a refusal by the script.

**Decision: the log is kept local, one file per train, in the git common directory**
(`<git-common-dir>/concrete-landing/log/<train>.tsv`). Train state lives next to it, in
`<train>/train.env`. The reasons:

- **Committing it to the train changes the candidate.** A row appended to the train after CI ran
  would land a SHA that CI never validated. The `land` row cannot exist before the land happens.
- **One shared TSV conflicts.** Every train would append to the same file, which recreates the
  ledger conflicts R-0542 removes. One file per train cannot conflict.
- **The common directory is shared by every worktree.** `report.sh` and `train.sh status` see every
  train of the clone from any of its worktrees; building, checking and landing run only in the full
  clone itself ("Where a train runs").

To publish the record, `report.sh --export docs/project/landing_log/` copies the per-train files
into the tree for a documentation-only commit. Per-train files never conflict on rebase. That
directory is what R-0547's generated median, next to the candidate table, will read.

## Readiness

A branch is **ready** when all of the following hold:

- it is on origin, named `wip/<name>`;
- its head commit is not in main;
- its head carries both notes below, published on origin.
  done
  valid_name "$name"
  stage_start
  local id; id="$(state_get "$name" run_id)"
  [ -n "$id" ] || die "watch: no CI run recorded for $name (train.sh ci $name)"
  local status="" concl="" sha="" waited=0
  while :; do
    read -r status concl sha <<< "$(run_field "$id" status conclusion headSha)" \
      || die "watch: cannot read run $id"
    [ -n "$status" ] || die "watch: cannot read run $id"
    [ "$status" = completed ] && break
    [ "$waited" -ge "$timeout" ] && { log_row "$name" watch incomplete "timeout after ${waited}s, status $status"; die "watch: run $id still $status after ${waited}s (a timeout is not a result)"; }
    note "run $id: $status ($(run_jobs "$id" | cut -f2 | sort | uniq -c | tr -s ' \n' ' '))"
    sleep "$interval"; waited=$((waited + interval))
  done
  local jobs; jobs="$(run_jobs "$id")"
  local bad_landing bad_campaign
  bad_landing="$(printf '%s\n' "$jobs" | awk -F'\t' '$1=="landing" && $2!="success"')"
  bad_campaign="$(printf '%s\n' "$jobs" | awk -F'\t' '$1=="campaign" && $2!="success" && $2!="skipped"')"
  # The verdict must describe THIS train: its exact head, with a non-empty landing tier. Otherwise
  # it is no verdict (bisect --ci consumes this exit status).
  local head; head="$(state_get_soft "$name" head)"
  if [ "$sha" != "$head" ]; then
    log_row "$name" watch refused "run $id at $sha"
    echo "train: run $id ran ${sha:0:10}, not the train head ${head:0:10}; it says nothing about this train" >&2
    return 1
  fi
  # awk reads all of its input: `| grep -q` under pipefail can SIGPIPE the writer and flip the test.
  if [ "$(printf '%s\n' "$jobs" | awk -F'\t' '$1=="landing"{n++} END{print n+0}')" = 0 ]; then
    log_row "$name" watch refused "no landing-tier jobs"
    echo "train: run $id has no landing-tier jobs; an empty set is not green" >&2
    return 1
  fi
  local base unmet; base="$(state_get_soft "$name" base)"
  required_notes "$base" "$head" "$jobs"
  unmet="$(unmet_required "$base" "$head" "$jobs")"
  if [ -n "$unmet" ]; then
    log_row "$name" watch incomplete "required checks unmet"
    echo "train: run $id does not satisfy $REQUIRED_FILE; a missing, skipped or cancelled required job is no verdict:" >&2
    printf '    %s\n' "$unmet" >&2
    [ -z "$bad_landing" ] && return 1
  fi
  if [ -n "$bad_landing$bad_campaign" ]; then
    note "jobs that did not succeed:"
    printf '%s\n' "$bad_landing" "$bad_campaign" | sed '/^$/d' | awk -F'\t' '{printf "    [%s] %-10s %s\n", $1, $2, $4}'
    local line jid jname jc
    while IFS=$'\t' read -r _t jc jid jname; do
      [ -n "$jid" ] || continue
      case "$jc" in failure|timed_out) ;; *) continue ;; esac
      echo "----- log tail: $jname (job $jid) -----"
      "$GH" api "repos/$REPO/actions/jobs/$jid/logs" 2>/dev/null | tail -n 40 || echo "(log unavailable)"
    done <<< "$(printf '%s\n' "$bad_landing" "$bad_campaign")"
  fi
  if [ -z "$bad_landing" ] && [ -n "$bad_campaign" ]; then
    log_row "$name" watch landing-green "only the campaign tier failed (run conclusion $concl)"
    note "ONLY THE CAMPAIGN TIER FAILED: every landing-tier job succeeded. The campaign gates"
    note "qualification (stage c), not merges; train.sh land $name may proceed. Record the campaign failure."
    return 0
  fi
  if [ -z "$bad_landing" ]; then
    log_row "$name" watch passed "run conclusion $concl"
    note "run $id green at ${sha:0:10}; next: train.sh land $name"
    return 0
  fi
  # failed only when a landing job failed; otherwise (cancelled, skipped, pending) it is no verdict.
  local res=incomplete
  if [ "$(printf '%s\n' "$bad_landing" | awk -F'\t' '$2=="failure"||$2=="timed_out"{n++} END{print n+0}')" != 0 ]; then res=failed; fi
  log_row "$name" watch "$res" "run conclusion $concl"
  echo "train: landing tier is NOT green ($res). Bisect: train.sh bisect $name --test '<failing gate>'" >&2
  return 1
}

# --- record: main's CI run names the run that qualified its exact SHA (R-0570) ---------------------
# A landing pushes main to a SHA that the train's CI run (on ci/<name>) already validated. The push
# starts CI on main at that SHA; its first job, "Landing record", runs this. When the record holds,
# every other landing-tier job of main's run is skipped, so main's run concludes success with a
# summary naming the qualifying run, instead of running everything twice or showing "cancelled".
# When it does not hold, nothing is skipped: main's run runs every job itself. So main is green

exec
/bin/bash -lc 'cat scripts/oracle/{lib,fetch,publish,ci_check,run}.sh scripts/land/required_checks.py scripts/checks/lib/git_sandbox.sh' in /tmp/claude-30035/-home-unbalancedparen-projects-concrete2/e2c54e65-31b5-4145-8101-c2d7b071b97e/scratchpad/codexfull/119381451a570461935b186ec3f62ac96b020125/tree
 succeeded in 0ms:
# Shared by scripts/oracle/{build,fetch,run}.sh: the lock reader and the artifact format.
#
# THE ARTIFACT is a gzip'd tar, made byte-reproducibly (sorted names, mtime 0, uid/gid 0, gzip -n):
#   bin/concrete      the oracle binary (its sha256 is the lock's binary_sha256)
#   std/              the standard library at the locked commit (the binary reads it at run time)
#   shell/flake.nix shell/flake.lock shell/lean-toolchain
#                     the tag's dev shell, so the C toolchain that compiles programs is the locked one
#                     (a directory of its own: `nix develop path:shell` copies only these to the store)
#   MANIFEST.sha256   `sha256sum` lines for every other file in the artifact
# Its own sha256 is the lock's artifact_sha256, and MANIFEST.sha256's own sha256 is the lock's
# manifest_sha256. An installed artifact is a directory with exactly these regular files (and their
# directories, nothing else: no symlinks); oracle_verify_install anchors the manifest in the lock, so
# every installed file (std/, shell/ as well as bin/concrete) is authenticated by the reviewed lock.
ORACLE_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
ORACLE_LOCK="$ORACLE_DIR/ORACLE.lock"
lock() { sed -n "s/^$1=//p" "$ORACLE_LOCK" | head -1; }
oracle_home() { echo "${ORACLE_HOME:-$HOME/oracle}"; }
oracle_artifact_name() { echo "oracle-$(lock tag)-$(lock platform).tar.gz"; }

# oracle_pack STAGE OUT: write the artifact for staged directory STAGE (which must hold the files above
# except MANIFEST.sha256, which this adds) to OUT.
oracle_pack() {
  local stage="$1" out="$2"
  ( cd "$stage" && rm -f MANIFEST.sha256 && find . -type f ! -name MANIFEST.sha256 | LC_ALL=C sort \
      | sed 's#^\./##' | xargs -d '\n' sha256sum > MANIFEST.sha256 ) || return 1
  tar --sort=name --mtime=@0 --owner=0 --group=0 --numeric-owner --format=gnu \
      --mode='u+rwX,go+rX,go-w' -C "$stage" -cf - . | gzip -n -9 > "$out"
}

# oracle_verify_install DIR: exit 0 iff DIR is an intact install (prints the reason otherwise).
oracle_verify_install() {
  local d="$1" want got
  [ -f "$d/MANIFEST.sha256" ] && [ -x "$d/bin/concrete" ] || { echo "no installed artifact at $d"; return 1; }
  [ ! -L "$d" ] || { echo "$d is a symlink"; return 1; }
  want="$(lock manifest_sha256)"; got="$(sha256sum "$d/MANIFEST.sha256" | cut -d' ' -f1)"
  [ -n "$want" ] && [ "$got" = "$want" ] || { echo "MANIFEST.sha256 is $got, the lock says ${want:-nothing}"; return 1; }
  # Only regular files and directories: a symlink (or fifo, device...) would escape the inventory.
  local odd; odd="$(cd "$d" && find . ! -type f ! -type d | head -1)"
  [ -z "$odd" ] || { echo "$d holds a non-regular entry: $odd"; return 1; }
  ( cd "$d" && sha256sum --quiet --strict -c MANIFEST.sha256 ) >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo "a file in $d differs from its manifest"; return 1; }
  # Nothing added: the files present are exactly the manifest's plus the manifest itself.
  local listed present
  listed="$( (sed 's/^[0-9a-f]*  //' "$d/MANIFEST.sha256"; echo MANIFEST.sha256) | LC_ALL=C sort)"
  present="$(cd "$d" && find . -type f | sed 's#^\./##' | LC_ALL=C sort)"
  [ "$listed" = "$present" ] || { echo "files in $d are not exactly the manifest's (added or missing)"; return 1; }
  want="$(lock binary_sha256)"; got="$(sha256sum "$d/bin/concrete" | cut -d' ' -f1)"
  [ -n "$want" ] && [ "$got" = "$want" ] || { echo "bin/concrete is $got, the lock says ${want:-nothing}"; return 1; }
}

# oracle_install TARBALL DEST: verify the tarball's sha256 against the lock, unpack, verify the install.
oracle_install() {
  local tb="$1" dest="$2" want got
  want="$(lock artifact_sha256)"; got="$(sha256sum "$tb" | cut -d' ' -f1)"
  [ -n "$want" ] && [ "$got" = "$want" ] || { echo "artifact is $got, the lock says ${want:-nothing}"; return 1; }
  rm -rf "$dest.new" && mkdir -p "$dest.new" && tar -xzf "$tb" -C "$dest.new" || { echo "cannot unpack $tb"; return 1; }
  oracle_verify_install "$dest.new" || return 1
  rm -rf "$dest.old"; [ ! -e "$dest" ] || mv "$dest" "$dest.old"
  mv "$dest.new" "$dest" && rm -rf "$dest.old"
}
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Fetch the published oracle artifact and install it, verified (ROADMAP R-0564; P13).
#
#   fetch.sh    download artifact_url from the lock, check artifact_sha256, unpack into
#               $ORACLE_HOME/<commit>/ and verify the install (manifest, nothing added, binary hash)
#
# This is how a runner other than concrete-ci-2 (a GitHub-hosted fallback runner) gets the oracle.
# The digest in the lock is what authenticates the download: the bytes are trusted because their
# sha256 is the one the reviewed lock names, whatever served them. build.sh is the independent
# check that those bytes are what the tag builds.
#
# Exit status: 0 installed and verified; 3 INCOMPLETE (no published artifact, download failed, or
# verification failed). A caller reports 3 as incomplete, which blocks landing when the oracle check
# is required; it never reports it as passed and never falls back to an unverified binary.
set -uo pipefail
HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
# shellcheck source=scripts/oracle/lib.sh
. "$HERE/lib.sh"
incomplete() { echo "oracle: INCOMPLETE: $*" >&2; exit 3; }
URL="$(lock artifact_url)"; WANT="$(lock artifact_sha256)"
case "$URL" in https://*) ;; *) incomplete "no published artifact (artifact_url is '${URL:-empty}')";; esac
[ ${#WANT} = 64 ] || incomplete "the lock has no artifact_sha256"
D="$(oracle_home)/$(lock commit)"
if oracle_verify_install "$D" >/dev/null 2>&1; then echo "oracle: already installed and verified at $D"; exit 0; fi
mkdir -p "$(oracle_home)" || incomplete "cannot create $(oracle_home)"
TMPF="$(mktemp "$(oracle_home)/fetch.XXXXXX")" || incomplete "no temporary file"
trap 'rm -f "$TMPF"' EXIT
curl -fsSL --proto '=https' --tlsv1.2 --retry 3 -o "$TMPF" "$URL" || incomplete "download of $URL failed"
why="$(oracle_install "$TMPF" "$D")" || incomplete "$why"
echo "oracle: fetched $URL, verified $WANT, installed at $D"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Publish the oracle artifact as a GitHub release asset (P13; owner approval required before the
# first run: it creates a public release). Authenticated by the caller's `gh` login.
#
#   publish.sh <artifact.tar.gz>
#
# Refuses unless the file's sha256 is the lock's artifact_sha256 (so only an artifact build.sh
# reproduced can be published) and its name is the lock's asset name. The release named by the lock's
# release_tag on the lock's release_repo is created with its tag AT THE LOCK'S commit (--target), never
# at the default branch's head; if the release already exists, its tag must resolve to that commit or
# publishing is refused. The asset is uploaded unless the release already holds it. Either way the
# published asset must download back to artifact_sha256. The lock's artifact_url is the result;
# commit it with the lock. Rerunning on a published release is safe (it only re-verifies).
set -euo pipefail
HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
# shellcheck source=scripts/oracle/lib.sh
. "$HERE/lib.sh"
die() { echo "publish: $*" >&2; exit 1; }
[ $# = 1 ] && [ -f "$1" ] || { echo "usage: publish.sh <artifact.tar.gz>" >&2; exit 2; }
WANT="$(lock artifact_sha256)"; GOT="$(sha256sum "$1" | cut -d' ' -f1)"
[ ${#WANT} = 64 ] || die "the lock has no artifact_sha256"
[ "$GOT" = "$WANT" ] || die "$1 is $GOT, the lock says $WANT"
REPO="$(lock release_repo)"; RTAG="$(lock release_tag)"; NAME="$(oracle_artifact_name)"
COMMIT="$(lock commit)"
[ -n "$REPO" ] && [ -n "$RTAG" ] || die "the lock has no release_repo or release_tag"
[[ "$COMMIT" =~ ^[0-9a-f]{40}$ ]] || die "the lock's commit '$COMMIT' is not a full commit id"
[ "$(basename "$1")" = "$NAME" ] || die "the asset must be named $NAME"
if gh release view "$RTAG" -R "$REPO" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  # The release exists: its tag must already name the locked commit.
  AT="$(gh api "repos/$REPO/commits/tags/$RTAG" --jq .sha 2>/dev/null)" || die "cannot resolve tag $RTAG on $REPO"
  [ "$AT" = "$COMMIT" ] || die "release $RTAG on $REPO is at commit $AT, the lock says $COMMIT"
else
  gh release create "$RTAG" -R "$REPO" --target "$COMMIT" --title "Legacy oracle $(lock tag)" \
    --notes "The frozen legacy compiler (tag $(lock tag), commit $COMMIT) as the differential oracle. sha256 $WANT. Rebuild and verify: scripts/oracle/build.sh."
  AT="$(gh api "repos/$REPO/commits/tags/$RTAG" --jq .sha 2>/dev/null)" || die "cannot resolve the new tag $RTAG on $REPO"
  [ "$AT" = "$COMMIT" ] || die "the new release $RTAG on $REPO is at commit $AT, not $COMMIT"
fi
# The asset names, one per line; matched as a whole line (no pipe into a short-circuiting grep).
ASSETS="$(gh release view "$RTAG" -R "$REPO" --json assets --jq '.assets[].name')" || die "cannot list the assets of $RTAG on $REPO"
if case $'\n'"$ASSETS"$'\n' in *$'\n'"$NAME"$'\n'*) true ;; *) false ;; esac; then
  echo "publish: $RTAG already holds $NAME; verifying it"
else
  gh release upload "$RTAG" "$1" -R "$REPO"
fi
URL="https://github.com/$REPO/releases/download/$RTAG/$NAME"
TMPF="$(mktemp)"; trap 'rm -f "$TMPF"' EXIT
curl -fsSL --proto '=https' -o "$TMPF" "$URL" || die "cannot download $URL"
[ "$(sha256sum "$TMPF" | cut -d' ' -f1)" = "$WANT" ] || die "the published asset does not download to $WANT"
echo "publish: $URL verified; set artifact_url=$URL in ORACLE.lock"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# THE ORACLE CHECK ON A GITHUB-HOSTED RUNNER (ISS-0005, P13; ROADMAP R-0564).
#
#   ci_check.sh    fetch the published oracle (fetch.sh: download, artifact sha256, install
#                  manifest, binary hash), then compile and run scripts/oracle/smoke.con through it
#                  (run.sh) and require its exit code, 42
#
# This is what proves that a runner other than concrete-ci-2 gets a verified oracle from the release
# alone. Exit status: 0 passed; 3 INCOMPLETE (no published artifact, a failed download, a digest or
# manifest mismatch, an install that fails verification: the oracle could not be trusted, so nothing
# was checked); 1 FAILED (the verified oracle ran and the smoke program did not exit 42). Both 1 and 3
# fail the CI job, so a required oracle check is never reported as passed without a verified oracle.
# A compile failure is FAILED whatever its status: run.sh announces it with "oracle: compile failed"
# on stderr, so a compiler (or nested nix) that happened to exit 42 is never taken for the program.
# It needs only bash and nix and sources nothing outside scripts/oracle, which it is retained with
# (RETENTION.tsv); CI runs it in the gate shell (check_gate_hygiene.sh GUARD_EXEMPT says why).
set -uo pipefail
HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
incomplete() { echo "oracle-check: INCOMPLETE: $*" >&2; exit 3; }
bash "$HERE/fetch.sh"; rc=$?
[ "$rc" = 0 ] || incomplete "fetch.sh exited $rc: the oracle is not installed and verified"
ERR="$(mktemp)" || exit 2; trap 'rm -f "$ERR"' EXIT
bash "$HERE/run.sh" "$HERE/smoke.con" 2>"$ERR"; rc=$?
cat "$ERR" >&2
[ "$rc" = 125 ] && incomplete "run.sh refused the install (it failed verification)"
if grep -q '^oracle: compile failed' "$ERR"; then
  echo "oracle-check: FAILED: the verified oracle did not compile smoke.con (status $rc)" >&2; exit 1
fi
[ "$rc" = 42 ] || { echo "oracle-check: FAILED: smoke.con exited $rc through the oracle, expected 42" >&2; exit 1; }
echo "oracle-check: passed: the published oracle fetched, verified, and ran smoke.con to exit 42"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Run one program through the pinned legacy oracle (scripts/oracle/ORACLE.lock).
#
#   run.sh [--interp] <file.con> [-- args...]   compile natively and run (default), or interpret
#   run.sh --path                               print the verified binary's path and exit
#
# The install ($ORACLE_HOME/<commit>/, from fetch.sh or build.sh) is verified before every use: each
# file against the artifact's manifest, nothing added, and bin/concrete against the lock's
# binary_sha256. Anything else is refused, never run. The compiler reads three environment variables
# (CONCRETE_STD, CONCRETE_MODEL_DIFF_PERTURB, PROOF_LAYER_MODE; git grep getEnv at the tag): the
# first is set to the verified std/ and the other two are removed, never inherited. The program is
# compiled and run inside the artifact's own dev shell (its flake.nix and flake.lock), so the C
# toolchain is the locked one.
#
# Exit status: the program's. A compile failure exits with the compiler's status after a line
# "oracle: compile failed" on stderr. 125 means the oracle itself is unusable (not installed, or
# failed verification): a caller must report that as incomplete, never as a program result.
set -euo pipefail
HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
# shellcheck source=scripts/oracle/lib.sh
. "$HERE/lib.sh"
die() { echo "oracle: $*" >&2; exit 125; }
D="$(oracle_home)/$(lock commit)"
why="$(oracle_verify_install "$D")" || die "$why (install with scripts/oracle/fetch.sh or build.sh)"
BIN="$D/bin/concrete"
[ "${1:-}" = "--path" ] && { echo "$BIN"; exit 0; }
mode=native
[ "${1:-}" = "--interp" ] && { mode=interp; shift; }
[ $# -ge 1 ] || { echo "usage: run.sh [--interp] <file.con> [-- args...] | --path" >&2; exit 2; }
FILE="$(cd "$(dirname "$1")" && pwd)/$(basename "$1")"; shift
[ "${1:-}" = "--" ] && shift
NIX=(env -u CONCRETE_MODEL_DIFF_PERTURB -u PROOF_LAYER_MODE CONCRETE_STD="$D/std"
     nix --extra-experimental-features "nix-command flakes")
OUT="$(mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/oracle-run.XXXXXX")"; trap 'rm -rf "$OUT"' EXIT
if [ "$mode" = interp ]; then
  "${NIX[@]}" develop "path:$D/shell" --command "$BIN" "$FILE" --interp -- "$@"
  exit $?
fi
"${NIX[@]}" develop "path:$D/shell" --command "$BIN" "$FILE" -o "$OUT/prog" >"$OUT/compile.log" 2>&1 || {
  rc=$?; echo "oracle: compile failed ($rc)" >&2; cat "$OUT/compile.log" >&2; exit "$rc"; }
"$OUT/prog" "$@"
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""THE REQUIRED-CHECK VERDICT FOR ONE CANDIDATE (train.sh watch and land).

Usage: required_checks.py BASE_FILE CAND_FILE TRANSITION_FILE JOBS_FILE
  BASE_FILE        required-checks.txt as the base (main) has it; empty file if absent
  CAND_FILE        required-checks.txt as the candidate has it; empty file if absent
  TRANSITION_FILE  required-checks-transition.tsv as the candidate has it; empty file if absent
  JOBS_FILE        the run's jobs, "<tier>\\t<conclusion>\\t<id>\\t<name>" per line (train.sh run_jobs)
  (a missing file and an empty file are the same: "the commit has no such file")

Prints one line per finding and exits 0; train.sh refuses when any line starts with an unmet kind:
  missing: <job>            a required job is absent from the run
  <conclusion>: <job>       a required job did not succeed (skipped, cancelled, failure, pending...)
  invalid: <reason>         the files themselves are unusable (nothing required, a bad transition row)
and prints notes that never refuse and never count as a pass:
  retired: <job> (<reason>) a base requirement the candidate retires with no replacement; NOT RUN
  replaced: <job> by <jobs> a base requirement whose replacements are required (and checked) instead
  stale: <job>              a transition row for a job the base no longer requires (delete the row)

THE RULE. The required set is the candidate's list plus every job the base requires, except a base
job the candidate drops through an explicit transition row. Without a row, a dropped base job stays
required, so a candidate cannot weaken main's requirements by editing its own list. A row is
`old_job<TAB>replacements<TAB>reason`: replacements is a comma-separated list of jobs that must
each be in the candidate's list (so they are required and checked at this exact SHA), or `-` to
retire the old job outright, which needs a reason and is reported as retired, never as passed.
A row for a job the candidate still requires is contradictory and refused. Files are read
separately and newline-terminated, so names from two files never fuse.
"""
import sys

UNMET = ("missing:", "invalid:")


def names(path):
    try:
        text = open(path).read()
    except OSError:
        return set()
    out = set()
    for line in text.splitlines():
        line = line.split("#", 1)[0].strip()
        if line:
            out.add(line)
    return out


def transition(path):
    rows, bad = {}, []
    try:
        text = open(path).read()
    except OSError:
        return rows, bad
    for n, line in enumerate(text.splitlines(), 1):
        if not line.strip() or line.startswith("#"):
            continue
        f = line.split("\t")
        if f[0] == "old_job":
            continue  # header
        if len(f) != 3 or not all(x.strip() for x in f):
            bad.append(f"transition row {n} needs old_job, replacements, reason (tab-separated, non-empty)")
            continue
        old, repl, why = (x.strip() for x in f)
        if old in rows:
            bad.append(f"transition row {n} repeats {old}")
            continue
        if repl == "-":
            rows[old] = ([], why)
            continue
        parts = [r.strip() for r in repl.split(",")]
        if not all(parts):
            # "a,,b" or "," must not degrade into a retirement: only a literal "-" retires
            bad.append(f"transition row {n} has an empty replacement name in {repl!r} (use '-' to retire)")
            continue
        rows[old] = (parts, why)
    return rows, bad


def plan(base, cand, rows, bad):
    """-> (required set, findings list)"""
    out = [f"invalid: {b}" for b in bad]
    if not base and not cand:
        out.append("invalid: required-checks.txt (neither the base nor the candidate has one)")
        return set(), out
    req = set(cand)
    for old in sorted(base):
        if old in cand:
            continue
        if old not in rows:
            req.add(old)  # dropped without a transition row: still required
            continue
        repl, why = rows[old]
        if not repl:
            out.append(f"retired: {old} ({why}); not run, not passed")
            continue
        missing = [r for r in repl if r not in cand]
        if missing:
            out.append(f"invalid: replacement {', '.join(missing)} for {old} is not in the candidate's required-checks.txt")
            req.add(old)
            continue
        out.append(f"replaced: {old} by {', '.join(repl)}")
    for old in sorted(rows):
        if old in cand:
            out.append(f"invalid: transition row for {old}, which the candidate still requires")
        elif old not in base:
            out.append(f"stale: {old} (the base does not require it; delete the row)")
    return req, out


def verdict(req, jobs_path):
    seen = {}
    for line in open(jobs_path):
        f = line.rstrip("\n").split("\t")
        if len(f) >= 4:
            seen.setdefault(f[3], []).append(f[1])
    out = []
    for name in sorted(req):
        c = seen.get(name)
        if not c:
            out.append("missing: " + name)
        elif any(x != "success" for x in c):
            out.append(",".join(sorted(set(c))) + ": " + name)
    return out


def main():
    if len(sys.argv) != 5:
        sys.exit(__doc__)
    base, cand = names(sys.argv[1]), names(sys.argv[2])
    rows, bad = transition(sys.argv[3])
    req, notes = plan(base, cand, rows, bad)
    for line in notes + verdict(req, sys.argv[4]):
        print(line)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# The sandbox helper (ISS-0015). Every script that builds throwaway repositories sources this and
# calls git_sandbox before its first git command:
#
#   source "<path>/scripts/checks/lib/git_sandbox.sh" || exit 2
#   git_sandbox                      # repository isolation only (keeps the caller's git config)
#   git_sandbox "$TMP/gitconfig"     # also isolates config: GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=<file>, no system config
#
# Why: git exports the variables that locate a repository (GIT_DIR, GIT_WORK_TREE, GIT_INDEX_FILE,
# GIT_COMMON_DIR, GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY, GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS and the rest) to the hooks it runs, and
# a hook of a LINKED worktree sees GIT_DIR=<repo>/.git/worktrees/<name>. A script started under that
# environment that runs `git -C "$sandbox" ...` acts on the CALLER's repository, not the sandbox:
# on 2026-11-06 the review-tool check, run by a pre-push hook in a linked worktree, created its test
# branches in the caller's clone, set core.bare there, and pushed 28 + 12 test notes to GitHub's
# refs/notes/concrete-review and refs/notes/concrete-review-findings. The list comes from git itself
# (`git rev-parse --local-env-vars`), so it tracks whatever the installed git treats as local.

git_sandbox() { # [gitconfig-file]
  local v vars
  vars="$(env -u GIT_DIR -u GIT_WORK_TREE -u GIT_COMMON_DIR git rev-parse --local-env-vars 2>/dev/null)" \
    || { echo "git_sandbox: git rev-parse --local-env-vars failed" >&2; return 2; }
  [ -n "$vars" ] || { echo "git_sandbox: git listed no repository-local variables" >&2; return 2; }
  for v in $vars; do unset "$v"; done
  if [ $# -ge 1 ]; then
    export GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL="$1" GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1
  fi
  return 0
}
